ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u4	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On 1 January 2017, the Public Collective Transport Act of 16 December 2010 will enter into force and related regulations which may pose a serious threat to the integrity of the public collective transport system in the country. The fundamental change resulting from the implementation of this law is the imposition on local government units of an obligation to organise public collective transport in their territory. Such a solution makes it the organiser - the governor, the governor, the mayor - and not, as so far, the entrepreneur, taking responsibility towards the public for the operation of collective public transport. Mostly due to the weak financial and organisational capacity of the majority of municipal and district authorities, the result of the introduction of this law may be a communications paralysis associated with the lack of access to collective communication of small villages and small towns, because no carrier will reach there due to the lack of relief and low profitability. This applies mainly to students commuting to schools, people with disabilities, pensioners, veterans. Minister, I have therefore a question as to whether the Ministry is planning to amend this law and whether or not it takes into account the delay in the implementation of this law in time. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u5	Thank you very much, Member. Please respond to Minister Jerzy Szmit.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u6	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! (PL) Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and The Law on public collective transport, which was adopted in 2010, is indeed making a very profound change in the organisation of collective transport. It introduces new institutions: on the one hand, transport operators and, on the other hand, the public transport organiser, to which the relevant government is to be responsible, as mentioned by the Member in her question. The local authorities have therefore been burdened with this decision to choose between the different operators, between the different companies which will be entitled to compensation for reduced tickets. Because it is as if the essence of this system, that these compensations will be granted and transferred only to those enterprises that will be selected by the local government. Today we have a different situation. In order for the company to receive compensation for reduced tickets, it is sufficient to meet three conditions, namely: the company should be authorised to carry out regular services, use registering banks and conclude a contract with the local government. Then the money that is a grant from the state budget is transferred by the voivode to local governments and goes to companies that make trips and drive people on the basis of reduced tickets. We are aware that if the law were to come in its entirety in the form it was adopted in 2010, it could indeed cause the risks that the Member mentioned. A large part of the Polish area, especially small towns, could be excluded by communication and exclusion could deepen. Companies that win tenders... At the moment, even when the law has not yet fully entered into force, local authorities are organising tenders for entrepreneurs and it is known that if there is a tender, there is usually more willing and some companies will lose these tenders and therefore will not carry out journeys. It must also be borne in mind that large, strong companies will win the tenders, which will be able to offer better conditions on their own. However, unfortunately, we are afraid of this, and I share these concerns that this will be at the expense of those small towns where there is little traffic in which there is no big profit to be expected, a large stream of passengers, as is said in the transport economy, and therefore these companies will rather strive to reduce travel. It may therefore be so that when the town is small, access to collective transport may be hindered. Because we see all these threats, we go against the Senate initiative - as I know it has already been submitted in the Senate today - an extension of the vacathio legis of this law, or at least a part of it, which concerns those cases I am talking about, about 1 year. In this respect, the Act would enter into force on 1 January 2018, if it were the will of the Senate and then of the High House. We would like to use these more than 1.5 years to introduce changes to the law on the risks that you have mentioned here and on which there are also many environments, consumer environments, business environments, transporters, but also local authorities. We want to discuss these issues and we want to propose to the High House the form of a law which will prevent these threats, so that the journeys can continue in an undistorted way from 2018 and that communication coherence is not distorted, communication cohesion has not deteriorated. On the other hand, I would also like to make it clear and clear with regard to the various information that appeared in the media space that there will be no, there are no threats today. All those who want to travel need to travel, who need to drive and who commute to work every day, to schools, doing their duties, or for other reasons, can enjoy the relief, all those who deserve it. Please wait calmly for the work of Parliament, the work of the government that we have discussed here will solve these problems. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u16	Mr. Marshal! Dear Messengers, Dear Members, First, I want to overthrow a myth that emerged during the public debate on this subject in the last two weeks. I don't know what that means. In the slightest suspicion... I'd like to suspect that this is only due to a lack of understanding of official documents. Well, April 15th this year. Minister of Culture and National Heritage Prof. Piotr Gliński announced the will to combine two museums telling about the history of World War II in Gdańsk, not to liquidate one of them, but to merge. So there's no intention of eliminating anything, there's only the will to merge. However, in order to understand where such a decision was made and what was in its background, one has to go back a little bit, and refer to history. Well, in 2006, 10 years ago, when Law and Justice ruled, the then Pomeranian voivode appointed a proxy to create the Westerplatte Museum. We thought that this place, commonly known to Poles as well as foreigners, a place of symbol, also commonly visited, because a lot of people come to Westerplatte, is not well exposed. In fact, there is nothing to see, also when it comes to getting acquainted with the details of this 7-day battle and with details of Polish resistance in 1939, it is also about exposing the artifacts that survived there. Therefore, we wanted to make such an attractive place, because this is also an attractive area for sightseeing, the outdoor and narrative museum was created for the anniversary of the outbreak of World War II in 2009. We had three years to do this, but a year later, the Civic Platform won power. At first it seemed that he would like to continue these plans that we had, because - I ask you very much to pay attention to this fact and remember this fact - the Minister of Culture in the Government of the Civic Platform Bogdan Zdrojewski on 1 September 2008, i.e. on a very symbolic day, created the Westerplatte Museum as a state institution of culture. Only after 3 months - apparently some discussion had to take place inside the governing party or government - the name of the Westerplatte Museum was changed to the World War II Museum and the story began that in Gdańsk the history of the whole World War II had to be presented in a universal way, with a Polish accent, of course, with a Polish narrative about this World War II, with Polish specificity. It was different, and there was this discussion between historians and publicists. They were presented with different concepts, they were modified. In any case, the Westerplatte Museum has been found not to exist, but to be the World War II Museum. And the Civic Platform government only established in January 2011 a multi-annual government programme for the construction of the II World War Museum, which was to be implemented until 2014. In 2014 we were to have an open World War II museum. The amount of PLN 358 million has been allocated in this long-term government programme, a rather amazing sum, because no programme, as far as the museums built in Poland are concerned - these new ones, built after 1989 - did not amount to such a sum. Probably only in the case of the Museum of the History of Polish Jews it was comparable, but I think it was also less, about 300 million PLN. Due to the terrible choice of a place to build this museum - in a port pool where a few meters thick concrete swimming pool had to be poured out, because it was considered that the museum was to be in the underground and not on the ground - in April 2015 the long-term government program was changed so that it turned out that the whole building would cost almost PLN 450 million. Adding to this almost PLN 50 million for the maintenance of the already existing museum, we have the sum of PLN 0.5 billion - investment and maintenance of this museum - already programmed in the state budget. Such a museum investment has not yet been so expensive in Poland. Of course, when it comes to the Westerplatte Museum, an attractive outdoor and narrative museum, it was estimated at PLN 80 million. That's six, seven times less. Let me put it this way: indeed, very much money has already been spent, very much money has been planned. We have a grudge against it, we have a right to have a grudge against it, and we have a right to see if that money was properly used. But the investment has really gone so far that it cannot be undone. That's what I'm saying very clearly. On the other hand, when it comes to caring about public funds, it is not for us to explain ourselves, but for the former rulers. Why would it cost so much? Why did it cost so much? Why should a Polish taxpayer spend PLN 0.5 billion on a museum? When we returned to power, we decided that we needed to return to the idea of the Westerplatte Museum and the War of 1939, and we set up this museum. We have estimated the costs, including those that I have just assessed in the Museum of World War II, and we have considered that, for organisational, institutional and financial reasons, it is better to combine these two initiatives, because it will be more efficient and more cost-effective, also when it comes to spending funds. So since this announcement came out, since April 14th, the Minister of Culture has been 3 months old. [[♪ The bell ♪]] to make an institutional and financial analysis of both museums and, after 3 months, to make a final decision on the institutional shape of these museums. I repeat: we are not closing anything, we are combining two initiatives. Thank you very much.

What was the result? Well, first of all, technology is actually very expensive. On the other hand, it concerns the indication of ultra-rare or ultra-rare indications, because when it comes to cancers of eye melanoma, the entry in the basket was earlier, while we considered the application for another four groups of medical indications. All these indications, as I repeat, are ultra-rare indications and therefore relate to less than one person in 50,000. residents. Therefore, of course, we could not apply the utilitarian approach, i.e. the ordinary economic analysis, and the reference of the cost of obtaining the health effect to the so-called. the cost-effectiveness threshold that is adopted for medicines. This technology had to be treated in a special way, but there was indeed a number of elements that had to be identified and deal with. Of course, we also see that Polish patients go to proton therapy abroad, especially in Prague, and therefore follow them, the modest summa summarum, the resources available to the NFZ. Therefore, of course, we wanted to make this procedure available to Poles as soon as possible. However, we had a few difficulties here. Firstly, the purchase and construction of the prototherapy facility and equipment together with the accelerator, with the cyclotron, were financed by scientific means, ladies and gentlemen. It was therefore necessary to consider what is the model, let us call it, the financing of this centre - whether only the National Health Fund should actually bear the costs of a prototherapy centre and whether research is also carried out there, and not only health services, and whether the fixed costs should be included in the calculations which concern the issue of the tariff or the creation of the valuation of this benefit. We have come to the conclusion that we should not take the fixed costs into account when using prototherapy tariffs, because they were financed from scientific sources, and that part of the functioning of the centre will also concern research and not the provision of health services in strict sense. In addition, we have had shortcomings, ladies and gentlemen, in terms of persons who would deal with the basket of guaranteed benefits. I would like to remind you that almost no one dealt with the basket of guaranteed benefits last year, but at the moment we actually have three high-end specialists at the Ministry of Health who are already starting to work on the basket, and we have already launched dozens of people from the National Health Fund branches. It is still not satisfactory to work on the basket, because these resources are still small, but in the end we already have these people. We had two more problems with the cab fare and putting it in the basket. Well, first of all, we obtained falsified cost data and had to verify it, second, there were reports in April and March that questioned the use of this method in the indication: glioblastoma multiforme and pericore cancers in children. We had to explain these doubts. As I say, they were explained, the tariff was announced 2 weeks ago, the entry in the basket will be made any moment, the therapy will be available from 1 June. Thank you very much.

Such provisions are the provisions of the Public Finance Act and the Public Procurement Act, which clearly indicate that special-purpose reserves can only be issued in situations where there is a legal basis for issuing these funds. That's the legal basis we don't have right now. Therefore, in order for these funds to be spent, such a legal basis should exist. ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u67	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, There are several very different legal problems in these questions. I'll try to refer to them. I would like to point out that the possibility for local governments to transfer schools to direct them to natural persons and legal persons other than local government units took place in 2009 by amending the Law on the system of education. In other words, then it was possible for the local government unit to transfer to a natural or legal person a school in which no more than 70 pupils attend. This transmission was possible after obtaining a positive opinion of the education curator. So I would like to point out that this type of action is undoubtedly further in need of such an opinion. I'm sure the current school curators will lean deeply on it. On the other hand, this provision further guarantees that this school remains accessible to the public, free of charge, that it is a public school. The transfer does not result in liquidation or a complete loss of influence of the local government. In other words, the government continues to subsidize it, it can preserve the circuit that was. The local government unit retains the right to monitor compliance with the conditions under which the school was transferred. The body that took over the school can't eliminate it freely, it can only give up running it. It is then re-acquired by the local government. As regards the activities that were undertaken in the previous period, i.e. in 2009 and 2015, 234 primary schools, nine secondary schools, two upper secondary schools were submitted at the time. Either way, it's 1.73%. primary schools, if you look at the aspect that functions in the education system. As regards the liquidation of schools, other activities were also undertaken, which consisted in the fact that the self-government units had liquidated their schools, while including them as public schools run by another body. As far as such activities are concerned, they have resulted in lower costs for the local government, because teachers were not employed on the basis of the Teacher's Charter at the time. In addition, the Supreme Audit Chamber pointed to the negative side of such actions, indicating that it was among others. The result was that the teacher's working time from the mandatory 18 hours was extended to 23 hours and the salary fell by approximately 40%. On the other hand, in the case of the changes that have taken place today, i.e. in accordance with the amendment of the Act on the Education System, or rather the Act of 29 December 2015, the powers of the school curator have been changed. They deal with the issue of school networks, and above all they deal with issues related to school closures. According to the provisions of 2009, i.e. from the previous period, the guardian's opinion was not mandatory in this case, but according to the provisions which entered into force on 23 January, this opinion issued in the form of a provision is mandatory. And now, referring to this issue, I would add that the actions of local governments stem from the gateway that was created by the 2009 amendment of the law, but also, you can say, a certain tactic of local governments, which indicated that schools should not be liquidated in the year in which local elections take place. In other words, the apogee of primary school liquidation took place in the school year 2008/2009, then 263 primary schools were missing, and the second critical moment was the school year 2012/2013, then 222 primary schools were lost. On the other hand, I would also like to say that the mere amendment and amendment of the law, the strengthening of the probation officer's powers and the entry into force of this on 23 January did not change the situation in itself, although the probation officer's orders became mandatory. In fact, this phenomenon of giving positive opinions was inhibited at the moment of changing the curators of education, so a little later. As far as combined classes are concerned, the rules currently allow for such organisation, which is the result of the 2001 Regulation. They allow the combination of classes in primary school, but also define the conditions, i.e. the rule speaks of particularly difficult demographic or geographical conditions. In fact, it is possible to combine classes in the 6-year-old pre-school ward and classes in Class I, while the school head is responsible for organising such classes, and the detailed rules govern them, in other words requiring the school head to adapt the framework curriculum to each of the combined classes so that the curriculum can be implemented. [[♪ The bell ♪]] As regards the issues involved, the Director also takes care of the students. At the moment, it is difficult for me to answer the question of how dynamic this process is. So far it has been clearly decreasing, i.e. in 2012 the combined classes represented 9.1% , in 2015 - 8,3%.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u78	Thank you very much. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u79	Dear Marshal, (PL) Mr President, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, Dear ladies and gentlemen, In order to clarify the fundamentals which lie within the scope of the powers of the national prosecutor, who by letter dated 27 April gave a written reply, I would like to point out that I am standing here with respect for the High House. There is a fundamental problem as to whether this answer lies in the question under Article. 196 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, because the basic thing is that the pardon procedure is governed by Article. 139 basic law and Article. 560.568 of the Code of Criminal Procedure. It must be stressed that the procedural actions undertaken by the competent authorities during the procedure for pardon are part of the activities covered by this Code of Criminal Procedure. Access to the file of the Attorney General in matters of pardon shall therefore take place by means of rules governing access to criminal proceedings, i.e. the standards of the Article. 156 Code of Criminal Procedure. As a result, the pardon procedure is a limited disclosure procedure in which certain documents are not even available to parties and other persons within the meaning of Article. 1 paragraph 1 Act of 6 September 2001 on access to public information. The pardon procedure is therefore specific in nature and concerns an individual case. It also has to do with the president's prerogatives. The transparency of this procedure, both at the stage of judicial and prosecutorial proceedings and at the stage of proceedings before the President of the Republic of Poland, is limited to the parties to the proceedings. I refer to the judgment of the Supreme Administrative Court of 5 March 2015, sygn. I OSK 1948/14. It is therefore authorised to believe that the pending pardon procedure does not fall within the normative scope of the concept of the current case within the meaning of Article. 196 resolutions of the Sejm. With regard to the matter under consideration in the present case, it should also be noted that according to Article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 2 Regulation No 1049/2001 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 30 May 2001 regarding public access to European Parliament, Council and Commission documents may be refused, inter alia. for the protection of legal proceedings. In conformity with Article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 3 of that Regulation, restrictions on access to public documents may include cases where a decision has not yet been taken by the institution if disclosure of such a document would seriously undermine the decision-making process, unless the public interest is in favour of disclosure. On the matter of formal law and the question of the Member. In the beginning, given the respect for the High House and the fact that the matter is undoubtedly of public interest, as the Member has already pointed out at the beginning, I kindly inform the Member and the High House that on 21 March 2016 the Minister for Justice - the Prosecutor General, acting in accordance with Article. 7 § Article 2 and Article 2 13 § 2 of the Act of 28 January 2016. The Law on the Prosecutor's Office gave my humble person, as Deputy Prosecutor General, an order to initiate ex officio pardon proceedings based on the content of the article. 567 § 1 of the Code of Criminal Procedure and suspension under Article. 568 Code of Criminal Procedure for Mr. Martin F. Penalty. By order of 22 March 2016, under the authority of the Attorney General, I initiated such proceedings on the basis of the conditions laid down in Article. 567 § 1 and Art. 568 Code of Criminal Procedure after hearing what is worth emphasising, with the request of the convicted defender of 28 January 2016 to the Public Prosecutor's Office of the President of the Republic of Poland. This concerns the conviction of Mr Marcin F. by judgment of 17 September 2015, ref. II K 915/14, by the District Court in Warsaw for the act of art. § 222 § 1 of the Penal Code and in no way in the content of the plea the personal data of the sentenced person have not been disclosed, [[♪ The bell ♪]] there was no plea of action in the recidivism, including Article. 64. Therefore, in addition to the fact that a pardon procedure was initiated, I applied for an opinion before the courts of both instances. Only after consulting the courts of both instances will the Attorney General decide to refer a position to the President in the exercise of the right of grace or not to exercise the right of grace, because such a right of the Attorney General has, and the mere fact that a pardon procedure is initiated does not prejudge the final decision in this regard. Thank you very much.

On the other hand, there is no non-disclosure clause of this procedure, because the non-disclosure clause, as we know exactly, is defined by the applicable law and defines the groups of documents which concern this issue. So we're dealing with an art. 156, or the mystery of conduct in a general sense, not in the sense of secrecy. Thus, there are no elements of secrecy that concern this particular procedure. Butter prices are falling - they are already at 10% lower than the current intervention price of EUR 2,40 per kg - and on the hard cheese market Polish products face strong competition. German and French cheeses are sold below all costs, their price is PLN 7.50 per kg. Under Polish conditions, the production of hard cheese at such a price would have to mean buying raw material, i.e. milk, below 60 gr. It should be pointed out that the abolition of milk quotas has had more disastrous effects than could have been imagined. In the absence of an appropriate market policy, the profitability of milk production is becoming increasingly threatened. ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u108	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! A good relationship between all links is essential for the proper functioning of the food supply chain. The structure of this chain makes it particularly vulnerable to unfair commercial practices. The problem of imbalance among the participants in the chain is not only present in Poland, but also in most countries of the European Union and is important due to the presence of highly consolidated entities in the processing sector, and especially distribution, against weaker, fragmented operators producing agricultural and food products. In the opinion of suppliers of agricultural and food products, the practice of forcing buyers, including in particular large-area shops, on providers of unfair contract terms, one-sided termination of these contracts, as well as forcing a number of additional payments which have nothing to do with the contract concluded or forcing them to purchase unnecessary services, unjustified extension of payment deadlines for the goods delivered. Unfair commercial practices are usually imposed in a situation of imbalance by the stronger side of the weaker side, and unfair practices at the level of food distribution weaken the financial condition of food producers and agricultural raw materials and thus reduce the quality of the goods produced, reduce the investment potential of food producers and reduce innovative activities. These factors have a negative impact on the consumer. Measures existing in the Polish legal system to counter unfair practices, although formally available without restrictions, are practically not used during contracts due to reprisals applied by buyers to suppliers who would undertake such an attempt. Although the problem concerns most European Union countries, the Community institutions have so far not taken effective action to defend suppliers, and most countries themselves under national legislation address this problem. A report from the European Commission has recently appeared indicating problems in this area. Unfortunately, Poland has been assessed as one of the countries in which this situation is the worst. Unfortunately, despite this report, no Community action is envisaged in this regard. Therefore, the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development has undertaken a draft regulation on this issue, a draft law regulating the area of food supply from producers of agricultural raw materials, through food producers, to the trade sector. We take into account the proposals made by producer organisations of the various industries, as well as the experience of other Member States. In the work on this bill in Poland, solutions adopted in other countries are used, but most of all national conditions are taken into account. The draft law will soon be addressed to a broad public consultation at all stages of the food chain in order to best adapt solutions to the expectations raised. The scope of the bill is also consulted on an ongoing basis, during meetings of the Ministry's management with industry and trade organisations and we want to forward this draft to the consultation in a short time, as soon as possible next month. The project will also cover the contracting agreements between farmers, agricultural producers and processors mentioned by your Member. Although this obligation was already introduced last year by the previous government, but since the deadline for concluding such an agreement has not been set and sanctions have not been laid down for the absence of such an agreement, this provision seems to be a dead provision. At the moment, in the form of surveys, we collect information from various organizations about how this law introduced last year works and whether our opinions on its low effectiveness are consistent with the facts. We will propose strengthening these mechanisms by introducing sanctions and, on the other hand, by setting deadlines for concluding an agreement. Under the current law, it may happen that when a farmer brings his product for sale, a contract is signed with the issuing of an invoice and this meets the requirements of the current law, so it is such a incomplete solution. At the same time, during meetings with representatives [[♪ The bell ♪]] We have proposed the development of a code of good commercial practices that goes beyond the scope of legislation. We've set up a team that's working on these kinds of solutions. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u214	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Prime Minister! I present the position of the Kukiz club15 in relation to the draft law amending the Act on the Institute of National Remembrance. The procedated bill on amending the Act on the Institute of National Remembrance - Commission for the Investigation of Crimes against the Polish Nationals provides for fundamental changes in the functioning of the IPN, which can be considered essential for its functioning. This is not the case, as some critics have said, that the new law is intended only to change the President of the Institute to one that will pursue historical policy and in general the majority of parliamentary policies, namely the Law and Justice. For when we look at the organisational and substantive solutions introduced, it will turn out that they can actually serve to dynamicise the functioning of this institution. First of all, attention should be paid to the temporary extension of the scope of the IPN activities. It is important to shift the cesuria of the initial research and organizational interests of the institute to 8 November 1917, because this indicates the origin of the totalitarian system and its criminal character directed against all Poles who love their homeland. Undoubtedly, the definition of the research and educational functions of the IPN should be regarded as a positive solution in the procedated law. It is good that they are linked together, showing that research does not serve anything without popularizing their results. The idea of creating proposals for formal and informal historical education is also important in this conjunction. A lot of reservations in the debate on the shape of the revision of the IPN Act were directed to the procedure for the election of the President of the IPN. The solution provided for in the Act will ensure its transparency and the choice of the person who will manage the obligations imposed on it by the Act. In this case, attention should be paid, for example, to the composition, members of the IPN College, to the criteria to be met, and the powers of that body in relation to the President of the IPN. The lack of an acute criterion, such as the issue of higher education by the members of the college, at this stage of the institution is not an obstacle at all, because the representatives of the historical environment who met it have so far been too organisational. and formally and even financially related to the institute. The impartiality of the college is guaranteed by the lack of such links. It is also important that it has the power to evaluate the research directions of the Institute. So far, this activity has been bound not only by the law, but also by the climate that it created. Therefore, seemingly controversial research areas related to the history of the extermination of Poles in the Eastern Kres were avoided. The research on Silk has obviously not been completed. Another point that should be highlighted concerns the organisational structure of the IPN. However, there may be doubts here. A mandatory set of organisational cells is foreseen in branches. This solution is generally beneficial, as not all areas of the country and the corresponding branches of the institute have the same needs for formal and substantive activities; it will certainly bring economic benefits. However, there is a danger that the President who has the power to appoint them will not notice certain needs in colloquial terms. The Act does not provide for such a change in the structure of the IPN, which would correspond to the administrative division of the country, which would undoubtedly facilitate the operation of its branches in the field. Some of the existing branches today carry out tasks far beyond the region in which the branch is located. The important thing is to pay attention to who might be an IPN employee in the act. The Act, thanks to the provision that an IPN employee may be a person who does not carry out activities incompatible with the activities of IPN, introduces the possibility of the functioning of trade unions in that institution. Unfortunately, the law does not meet the expectations of researchers of the Polish past in relation to at least two issues. The first is the total liquidation of a reserved collection, otherwise called a separate one. The envisaged temporary moratorium on its liquidation and its principles will undoubtedly lead to an even more restricted set of documents. The question is, why? The wordings set out in the amendment of the Act will allow for the preservation of the reserved set, moreover, an instrumental approach to the classification of documents for this set. Why 25 years after the liquidation of the document-producing institutions [[♪ The bell ♪]] Is this freakish thing going on? Nevertheless, changes are really going in the right direction. On this occasion it is also worth mentioning the procession itself, which shows that the PiS listens to criticism and adopts solutions that improve the original idea. ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u274	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Seym! The proposal for a law amending the Act of 11 October 2013 on special arrangements for the protection of jobs and the Act of 13 July 2006 on the protection of employees' claims in the event of insolvency of the employer is intended primarily to align the existing Act on special arrangements relating to the protection of jobs with the legal status which is in force in connection with the entry into force of the Act of 15 May 2016 and to correct the manifest mistake of the writer in the Act on the protection of employees' claims in the event of the insolvency of the employer. The following changes are proposed: firstly, as a result of the separation of insolvency proceedings and restructuring proceedings into separate legal acts, i.e. the Act of 28 February 2003 on insolvency and restructuring law, it is necessary to replace in the relevant provisions of the Law on special arrangements for the protection of jobs appeals to the provisions of the Act on insolvency and resolution by appeals to the provisions of the Act on insolvency respectively. In addition, the draft amendment aims to align the provisions of the Law on special arrangements for the protection of jobs with the Act on insolvency in connection with the repeal of the article. 12 of this law, it concerns bankruptcy and resolution law, by Article. 428 Restructuring Law. A new definition of insolvency as set out in Article. 11 ww. Insolvency laws have taken into account economic considerations for determining the financial situation of entrepreneurs. In the context of the so-called liquidity insolvency of the trader, the article was deleted. 12 Bankruptcy and resolution law as a whole, whereas Article. Article 12 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 in the version previously in force has not been transferred to the Restructuring Law. The explanatory memorandum to the draft Restructuring Law states that the loss of the ability to regulate liabilities should result from an assessment of the financial condition of the debtor's business. Insolvency should not be demonstrated by the fact that the debtor, e.g. for reasons beyond his control, he is unable to repay the debt at any time. The current insolvency law system protects market participants from a debtor who has lost his ability to regulate his debt for financial reasons. However, it should not be the aim of the legislator to introduce market elimination opportunities for entities which do not regulate their obligations due to reasons other than lack of liquidity. Proposed amendment of the Article. Article 23 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 The 11th Act on the Protection of Employee Claims in the event of the insolvency of the employer is intended to indicate the appropriate reference to the article. 23a ww. Act as it stands. It is proposed that the provisions of the proposed law enter into force 14 days after the date of publication. The print project No 362 entered the Sejm on 8 March, and on 18 March the Marshal of the Sejm addressed it to the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy, which at its meeting on 12 April welcomed it without opposition and recommended that the High House adopt it as in print No 412. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u276	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the parliamentary draft law amending the Law on special arrangements for the protection of jobs and the Act on the protection of employees' claims in the event of insolvency of the employer, print no. 362 and 412. Discussion of the changes to the legislation cited at the outset is an opportunity to take a broader look at the problem of lack of remuneration for the work done. I would like to stress that the law and justice in this House have repeatedly raised the problem of not paying for the work done, which has often been a drama for many workers. Unfortunately, our conclusions remain unnoticed. This is under the rule of the Civic Platform and the Polish People's Party of the employer in a general manner legalized with payment of salaries for the total amount of almost PLN 190 million. However, remuneration for work is the basis for the existence of most Polish families, therefore it is an element of social order, which from the point of view of law and principles of social relations should be subject to special protection. The employee's remuneration is protected not only by labour law but also by civil, administrative and criminal law. Here we have a question about the scale of this phenomenon. According to the State Labour Inspection data in the first half of 2015, there were more than 30,000 cases of non-payments to employees. Non-payments are most often made in small and medium-sized enterprises, such as construction, trade and industrial processing. Of course, employers, without paying their employees' wages, tend to explain the company's economic problems. This kind of practice is not our agreement. For failure to pay the employer's salary, there is a fine of 1 000. Up to 30,000. zł. In the first half of 2015, the labour inspectors imposed almost 3 000. fines for employers who did not pay their wages or lower them. They also sent about 580 applications to court to punish employers who committed this offence. ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u290	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I was honoured to present to the High House the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice regarding the draft law which aims to transpose into the Polish legal order Directive 2014/54/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 April 2014 on measures to facilitate the exercise of rights granted to workers in the context of the free movement of workers in the field of the Polish rules which have not been regulated so far. I would like to present here in a few sentences the High House, as set out at the outset in the implementation directive. The aim of the Directive is to make it easier for mobile workers to exercise the rights granted under Article. 45 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union and Regulation No 492 of the European Union of 2011 More generally, the aim of the Directive is to define the measures that Member States should apply in order to facilitate migrant workers in the internal market and their family members' rights under the rules on free movement of workers. These measures include, inter alia, ensuring appropriate procedures enabling workers and their family members to pursue judicial claims related to the unjustified limitation of their rights, the introduction of measures to combat victimisation, i.e. harming someone, in connection with complaining or supporting dialogue with the social partners. Those measures should be applied in respect of the powers laid down in Regulation No 492/2011 mentioned earlier. The following legal acts concern the Polish legislation: the Labour Code Act, the Employment Promotion Act and the Labour Market Institutions Act, the Social Security Act, the Family Benefits Act and the Individual Income Tax Act, as well as the Social Assistance Act. I would like to draw your attention to the House that most of the obligations imposed by the Directive are already being implemented in the above-mentioned legislation, especially under labour law and social security law, as well as in the procedural rules on complaints and redress. Social dialogue also has its proper legitimacy in the Polish legal system and is conducted in cooperation with the social partners to combat unjustified restrictions and obstacles to the right to free movement and discrimination on grounds of nationality in relation to EU workers and their family members. Social dialogue and its promotion are within the scope of the Social Dialogue Council, which works in Poland to build a social agreement and regular dialogue between workers' and employers' organisations and government parties, and supports social dialogue at all levels of local self-government units. At this point, it should be stressed that during the governments of the coalitions of PO and PSL, especially in 2012 and 2015, the social dialogue did not function and the Tripartite Commission for Economic and Social Affairs was a dead creation. As a reminder, in October 2015, President Andrzej Duda appointed members of the Social Dialogue Council - a new forum for cooperation between government representatives, employers and employees. Mr Piotr Duda is the chairman of the Council for the coming year, counting from the date of its appointment. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In examining the bill submitted to transpose into the Polish legal order Directive 2014/54/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 April 2014 on measures to facilitate the exercise of rights conferred on workers in the context of the free movement of workers, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, I state that Poland is prepared to implement the Directive. In conclusion, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the proposed bill by requesting its adoption. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

However, this is a very important decision given the high number of Polish citizens working in other countries of the European Union. ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u296	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I am pleased to present my position on the report of the committee on the Government draft law amending certain laws in connection with the implementation of Directive 2014/54/EU of 16 April 2014 on measures facilitating the exercise of rights conferred on workers in the context of the free movement of workers, printing Nos 431 and 454. The solutions adopted implement Directive 2014/54/EU, which indicates the measures to be provided by the Member States of the European Union to citizens of the European Union and the European Economic Area and their family members in order to facilitate the exercise of their rights under the free movement of workers. In practice, the implementation of the Directive is intended to contribute to raising awareness among citizens of the European Union and the European Economic Area of their powers in other Member States and, if necessary, to facilitating their investigation. All countries of the European Union are obliged to carry out the tasks set out in the Directive so that all mobile workers can benefit from the powers set out therein. Most of the tasks mentioned in the Directive are already being carried out by Poland. Currently, it is only necessary to designate an institution - one or more - which will carry out tasks related to the promotion of the rights of persons enjoying the free movement of workers, provide the necessary information on the labour market, provide independent legal advice, and conduct or commission independent research and publish independent labour market reports. According to the draft amendment, the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy will promote equal treatment of EU workers in our country, in particular by: commissioning independent surveys and analyses and publishing independent reports and recommendations on unjustified restrictions and obstacles to the right to free movement and discrimination of workers, initiating and monitoring measures to combat unjustified restrictions and obstacles to the right to free movement and discrimination of workers, publishing information on the rules on the free movement of workers and their application in Poland, acting as a contact point for cooperation and exchange of information with other Member States on the free movement of workers. The draft amendment also broadens the scope of legal advice provided by the State Labour Inspection. Until now, workers from EU countries and members of their families in Poland have received legal advice on labour law and access to employment from the State Labour Inspection. After the changes, they will also be able to count on assistance on issues such as access to social and tax privileges, membership of trade unions, access to housing resources, access to education, vocational training and vocational education for children, assistance provided by labour offices. In order to ensure that the legal advice provided by the State Labour Inspection is reliable, it will be able to address public institutions, e.g. to ZUS, tax offices or tax offices, requesting information necessary to clarify individual cases reported by employees. The Polish People's Party Club is in favour of the proposed bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u302	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Marshal of the Sejm referred this project to the first reading in the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, which took place on 13 April. The Committee on Agriculture set up a subcommittee which examined the draft law on 26 April and adopted the report. The project concerns amendments in fact in three laws: in the Act of 17 December 2004 on the Registration and Protection of the Names and Designations of Agricultural Products and Foods and on Traditional Products, in the Act of 21 December 2000 on the Commercial Quality of Agricultural and Food Products and in the Act of 25 June 2009 on Organic Farming. Draft Print Act No 385 and 450 is intended to align these laws with Regulation No 1151/2012 of the European Parliament and of the Council on quality schemes for agricultural products and foodstuffs. The amendment amends the application procedure for registration of protected names, geographical indications and traditional specialities guaranteed. The deadlines for examining applications are reduced and the catalogue of tools for supervising the activities of control bodies has been completed. The duties of the chief inspector of the commercial quality of agri-food products and the principles of cooperation with the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection have also been clarified. This will improve the efficiency of the system of official controls and thus protect names more effectively on the market. In addition, the main inspector for the commercial quality of agri-food products is required to publish on the website a list of producers holding a valid quality certificate or a valid certificate of conformity. The proposal also proposes to replace too sensitive criminal rules on the incorrect use of names registered with financial penalties imposed by a regional inspector of the commercial quality of agri-food products or a regional inspector of commercial inspection, given the different circumstances, of course. ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u306	Mrs. Marshal! Mr Fellowships, Mr Fellow Members, I have the honour to present on behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club a position on the report on the government draft law amending the Act on registration and protection of the names and indications of agricultural products and foodstuffs and on traditional products and certain other laws. The purpose of the Act amending the Act on registration and protection of agricultural products and foodstuffs and on traditional products and certain other laws is to adapt the Polish legal order in this respect to Regulation No 1151/2012 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 21 November 2012 on quality schemes for agricultural products and foodstuffs. The draft law also takes into account certain changes resulting from the practice which, according to the applicant, aim to improve the system for the protection and registration of geographical indications of agricultural products and foodstuffs. The surveillance system and the efficiency of official controls of the protected designations of origin, protected geographical indications, traditional specialities guaranteed by the Agricultural and Food Quality Inspection is expected to be strengthened, inter alia. by allowing inspections to be carried out on agricultural products in the event of the placing on the market of products produced which are not in conformity with the registration. In order to ensure the transparency of the system and to facilitate contact between the manufacturer and the consumer, the principal inspector of the commercial quality of agri-food products will be required to publish on the website of the inspection a list of producers holding a valid quality certificate confirming the conformity of the production process of the agricultural product and the foodstuff with the specifications. The proposal also provides for fines to be imposed by the Chief Inspector for the commercial quality of agri-food products on certifying bodies in the event of non-compliance with the applicable law. The draft law will mainly affect producers of agricultural products and foodstuffs who will in the future apply for registration of the names of their products and foodstuffs, but has not been submitted to such more detailed consultations and arrangements with the representatives of the stakeholders. However, it must be stressed that the participation of producers in the EU system of protected designations of origin and protected geographical indications and traditional specialities guaranteed is voluntary and is decided individually by each producer. Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, In a peasant way, this bill helps all of us, customers and consumers to do it. certain, safe shopping. We buy, looking at the labels, what is written there from the point of view of reliability and reliability of the record. If that's the case, we all know in our country. This bill will change that. Obviously, none of us want to pay for beautiful labels. The Act gives broad powers to the Trade Quality Inspection of Agricultural and Food Articles in terms of production control, criteria control, certification as well as penalties, as I mentioned earlier. This is a step towards taking the so-called products as consumers. Swiss trust, and with him here, as I mentioned earlier, is not good. Being in the European Union makes it possible to benefit from the acquis of the Union in the field of good law. This document serves good and reliable, and above all our health. That is why we should be very careful to be in the European Union. I would like to tell you that I am very happy with this bill, because this law will certainly introduce a certain order. This will not happen immediately, but I think that, as in other countries - Austria, Germany, Switzerland, France - we, consumers, will gain good and high confidence in the products we will buy. This bill, this bill is going in the right direction, which is why the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club will vote for it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u308	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The draft amendment to the Act on the Registration and Protection of the Names and Designations of Agricultural Products and Foods and on Traditional Products and on the amendment of certain other laws, submitted by the Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development, introduces new provisions on geographical indications of agricultural and food products. These amendments are related to the implementation of European Union rules in Polish law, namely Regulation (EU) No 1151/2012 of the European Parliament and of the Council on quality schemes for agricultural products and foodstuffs. It refers to solutions common to all Member States of the European Union contained in Regulation No 882/2004 on official controls performed to confirm compliance with feed and food law, animal health and welfare rules. The introduction of the Regulation aimed at reducing the duration of EU procedures, as the process of marking was several years. The revision of the rules is also contained in Special Report No 11/2011 of the European Court of Auditors, as well as in the Audit Report of the European Bureau. Food and Veterinary. The project envisages strengthening the supervision and control system of the chief inspector of the commercial quality of agri-food products both over authorised certification bodies and on producers of products. In Chapter 9, the title of which is changed to the title of "Criminal penalty' - Article. 57 - the extent of irregularities in the activities of certification bodies and producers of products which are subject to a monetary penalty shall be mentioned. Chapter 9 of the Act currently in force focuses on producers of products and also allows for a restriction or deprivation of liberty. In the new version of this chapter, the rules only sanction fines as revenue of the State budget, making them subject to the degree of harmfulness of the act, the extent of the infringement, the activities of the infringer so far and the amount of revenue. The tasks of the principal inspector of the commercial quality of agri-food products shall be to publish information on producers and their products which have Union markings, thereby facilitating their recognition and access to the knowledge of products registered under the protected designation of origin, protected geographical indication and traditional specialities guaranteed. We hope that this will affect the building of consumer awareness and the creation of a positive image of our domestic products outside Poland. In addition, it will be possible to control the main inspector of the commercial quality of agri-food products in producers who market products which do not comply with the registered specification under registered names or names referring to them, i.e. falsified. In terms of supervision, transmission of information on producers and marketing of the products concerned, the Chief Inspector shall cooperate with the Commercial Inspection and the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection. Other assumptions introduced by the Act of amendments are to improve the functioning of the system of protection and registration of geographical indications of agricultural and food products, i.e. protection against unlawful use of registered names in the case of products which do not meet the requirements of the specification, increasing the readability and efficiency of the system, guaranteeing the quality of the regional products placed on the market, as well as concreteising the provisions of the current law. Easier said, it's good for consumers to know exactly what they eat. We support the bill because of the alignment of Polish law with EU standards in this area. EU labels support the protection of regional traditions, and ensuring the quality of regional products can be included in one of the development paths of Polish agriculture which is a kind of promotion for the regions of Poland. This is a good project we'll support. Thank you. [[Applause]]

A great friend and defender of Polish English writer Gilbert Chesterton once wrote, almost 100 years ago, because in 1922, the following words, I quote: My instinctive sympathy for Poland was born under the influence of constant accusations against her and, I may say, I made myself a court of Poland on the basis of her enemies. Every time I met an individual with a slave soul practicing usury and a cult of terror, meddling in the swamp of materialistic politics, I discovered so many times in this individual, alongside the above properties, a passionate hatred for Poland. Chesterton points out that the Polish case has always powerful in the world of enemies, and that is why it must be defended. Chesterton points out that the values that constitute the center of Polishness are generosity and valor. The strengthening of Polish citizens, training of collective character, community education focused on a particular attachment to the issue of freedom and solidarity, heroism and nobility is a special task for everyone who loves his homeland. Following this lead, indicated by Chesterton, we come to the first and most important, if not at all the only reason why, finally after 27 years, we can present in the Seym a historical draft law on national media. Why hasn't there been a bill like this so far? Why did it take so long for it to be passed? Why is its adoption a sacred duty of Polish patriots? Today, social media, especially social media owned by the nation, play an unimaginably great role in building a national and national community, constitute a fundamental and irreplaceable source of light radiating to other countries and nations, guaranteeing national sovereignty. This is an additional socially strong border of security for the freedom of all of us. Polish Television, Polish Radio, 17 regional radio stations of Polish Radio, finally Polish Press Agency, together with 20 institutions, according to our project they create a consistent and stable media system with a widely defined mission. For the first time in the definition of national media missions, we have presented the recording of many fundamental obligations building up the strength of the nation and the state. I quote the proposed article. 3 (1) (a) 2 Laws: The public mission of national radio and television institutions is to acquire and disseminate reliable information from Poland and abroad, to cultivate national traditions and values of patriotic and humanities, to contribute to meeting the spiritual needs of listeners and viewers, to raise and satisfy their comprehensive interests, to disseminate the achievements of Polish and world science and artistic creativity, to promote various forms of civic activity, to facilitate access to objective information and to create conditions for a pluralistic debate on public matters - by offering the public and its various parts of diverse radio and television programmes and other information services, publication, culture, education, sport and entertainment with the principles of impartiality, pluralism, independence and high quality. In addition, in the article. 4 of the proposed law we prejudice that in their messages the institutions of national media are to take care of the culture of Polish language, to maintain high, professional and ethical standards of journalism and to ensure the quality of communication in terms of content and technology. These institutions will, in accordance with the law, respect the Christian system of values, taking as the basis the universal principles of ethics. Other tasks related to the performance of the public service mission have also been written down in detail. And so, firstly, obtaining and disseminating reliable information from Poland and abroad, secondly, strengthening the national community and strengthening responsibility for the common good, thirdly, enriching historical awareness and countering the distortion of the image of Poland's history, which I consider to be a particularly important task of national media. The dissemination of knowledge about Poland abroad, the reliable demonstration of the diversity of events and phenomena in Poland and abroad, the promotion of the free development of citizens' views and the formation of public opinion, the opportunity for citizens and their organisations to participate in public life by presenting diverse views and positions and exercising the right to control and social criticism, supporting citizens in the exercise of the right to public information, demonstrating the value of family life and promoting family life, contributing to the comprehensive development of the young generation and the protection of children and young people from demoralisation, serving the development of science, education and culture, with particular regard to Polish intellectual and artistic achievements, including support of native creation, dissemination of knowledge about Polish language, documentation of radio and television works and events of major importance in Poland's history, the archives of institutions being their property - this is a very important statement - and meeting the needs of Polish and Poles abroad in terms of access to information about Poland and ties with their homeland. Special missionary tasks will also include taking into account the information and cultural needs of national and ethnic minorities living in Poland, as well as taking into account the special needs of people with disabilities. The list of national media tasks recorded will also include: shaping health-promoting attitudes, promoting environmental awareness, promoting sport, promoting attitudes and initiatives to combat social pathologies, promoting media literacy, promoting professional and ethical standards in the media and the qualifications of people employed in them, cooperating with state and local authorities, the Catholic Church, other churches and religious unions, public benefit organisations and organisations and institutions, including those active among Poles and Poles abroad, for the common good and good of people. The Act guarantees editorial independence of national media institutions. Their tasks will be formulated in the financial and programme plans adopted and evaluated respectively by the National Media Council and having a broad civic representation of the social programme councils, because socialisation and separation of new institutions of national media from current political influences and from executive authority is one of the pillars of the present law. This bond will be a civil bond and a bond based on the responsibility of journalists and media managers before a nation paying a contribution, not against individual political parties or gracious parties - current or future - the finance minister. Everyone involved in media funding will have the full right to expect good use of their money to carry out a broad but very specific mission. ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u320	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen of the Minister! I have the honour to present the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice Act on audiovisual contribution. Ladies and gentlemen, I will start by saying that for many years Poland has been discussing how to finance public media, how to finance them, how to operate, how to account, and what is in the programme. And for many years as an environment referring to the fact that public media are a value, one of the pillars of statehood, we have said that it is important to ensure that public media must have a programme which is a positive programme, shaping patriotic, social, civic attitudes; this is what the previous speaker has said. But we also talked about the fact that the state must support this kind of mission through a contribution that will be a contribution valid for all citizens, not just for a group of responsible citizens who have paid their previous subscription. Unfortunately, most citizens did not pay this subscription. The level of payment of the subscription fell dramatically after a politician appeared in the Polish political space, who called this contribution a racket and said that it was a scandal that such a racket existed, and that immediately after it came to power this racket would end. And what happened when that politician came to power? Well, eight years of his formation... He himself was prime minister for nearly seven years and did not terminate the subscription. Moreover, when he encouraged not to pay a radio-television subscription, the Poles who listened met with severe punishment. Turns out that's what the bailiff was downloading. Today, ladies and gentlemen, there has come a moment where we say that this form of subscription is indeed an archaic form. Here we agree with the former Prime Minister, but we do not agree with the fact that it is necessary to completely eliminate the financing of this by the citizens. But it has to be a common contribution, it has to be a fair contribution, it has to be a small contribution, so it's a poor contribution, but it's quite commonly paid. There was a discussion on how to implement this in practice, because the voluntary payment of the radio-television subscription proved ineffective, and to a large extent because of this policy, which I have already quoted here. Well, what to do when citizens do not pay voluntarily, they are encouraged not to pay, but at the same time require some kind of effort: writing, writing, sending transfers, etc.? In many countries, as well as now in this proposal, a simple mechanism has been adopted: electricity consumers who have meters - in the 21st century it is difficult to imagine that there are households in which neither radio nor television - pay such a flat rate, an audiovisual contribution simply on the bill. It is uncursed, easy to download, inexpensive, and at the same time thanks to the fact that it will be a common contribution, it can be much smaller. The project, which we are submitting to the High House, proposes a contribution of PLN 15 per meter. But we can think about it. We will also lean on opposition and citizens' arguments. Perhaps we can find a way to power public media, while minimising the burden for citizens as much as possible. Currently, the contribution proposed in this bill is 1/3 lower than the existing subscription, and for all those who honestly supported public media in Poland, this will be a decisive reduction. We understand that those who have avoided paying, for various reasons, may be dissatisfied, because now it will be harder for them to avoid paying this contribution, but for honest citizens who feel obliged to pay this contribution, it will be a reduction in costs. For those who, in order not to use a word that could be considered unelegant, avoided paying, so to put it, it will indeed be an increase. But since it's not a high contribution, I don't think it's going to be very severe. Although in this law we have also foreseen, especially for those who are in difficulty and for seniors - as in the previous period, for the existing subscription fee - exemptions. We want the exemption to apply to seniors after 75 years of age, so that it also applies to various units subordinate to local governments, so that it is simply that those who are in a difficult situation also have the opportunity to be exempted from this obligation, i.e. to relieve them of their financial difficulties. Ladies and gentlemen! So, ladies and gentlemen, in summary, we encourage a fair and factual debate on these projects, a departure from criticism. At the same time, I would like to remind you that for 8 years these most fierce critics who say that this should be eliminated or that this is a bad solution when they were seeking power, said that the subscription fee was an archaic racket, for 8 years of this archaic racket did not terminate, and today, when Law and Justice propose to modernise this contribution and lower its amount for citizens, they are very fierce critics of this form, one can say, the implementation of their programme, namely the elimination of the broadcasting subscription and the replacement of it with a low audiovisual fee. In addition, ladies and gentlemen, this fee will be directed to a special fund, to an account that will serve public media, will be separated from the state budget, so it will not be what our opponents are claiming, that it will be some form of government interference, because this contribution will be unrelated to the state budget, so it will be independent and guarantee the independence and stability of public media. Ladies and gentlemen! I am very pleased to present and submit this proposal to the High House. I'm counting on the points. We are open to various kinds of amendments, because even during this debate I have heard about some interesting ideas that could make the bill better in implementation. This is the kind of cooperation I look forward to, welcome and thank you very much for your attention.
