ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u322	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! For years, the public debate has raised the need for changes in the regulation of public media. A comprehensive reform is expected to improve the functioning of public radio and television to serve the real needs and interests of society. The government coalition has announced this many times. We did not see, although the National Radio and Television Council sent to the Ministry of Culture analysis and assumptions proposed for laws. The National Council assessed that in recent years two phenomena are characteristic for the Polish media market: firstly, oligopolization and capital concentration, secondly, tabloidisation, i.e. the process of transforming almost all the content of the media so as to fulfil the entertainment function, which limits their information function and very narrows the picture of public life and involves abandoning serious analysis and discussion and thus deprives the recipient of the possibility of understanding the phenomena. A counterweight to these processes can and should be strong public media, non-commercial and high-quality information and programme-oriented, such as integrate rather than disintegrate society, promote full representation of all important social forces and fair participation in public life. In Poland today we are at a historic moment where we have the opportunity to build good solutions for public media, which will enable us to achieve this goal. Reform is needed here, which will lead to a stable legal environment and transparent procedures. It's a challenge. At the beginning of the 19th century, President John Adams wrote: If there is ever going to be an improvement in the fate of humanity, philosophers, theologians, legislators, politicians and moralists will see that the most difficult and most important problem that they will have to solve is the creation of appropriate legislation regulating the functioning of the press. Today, of course, not just the press. It should also be remembered that public media cannot be debated in isolation from the context in which they operate. It is important to remember that the crisis of public media in Poland led to the fact that, firstly, many politicians ceased to care about public media, it can be said that politicians who ruled Poland after 2008 did, unfortunately effectively, a real attack on public radio and television, realizing the goal of their maximum weakening, as declared by Donald Tusk, because just as the governments of the PO-PSL rolled up the state, unfortunately the media, and secondly, the social awareness of the particular role of public media for the functioning of the state, weakened effectively by commercial media and these politicians, followed by a lack of concept and inconsistencies of media policy of the Third Republic. At the same time, it is not possible to demand from the public media a missionary program and not to provide financial conditions that would make the public broadcaster independent of commercial pressures. And the public media were brought to commercialize. This was decided in particular by the funding structure that makes the programme dependent on advertising revenues and forces us to take into account the interests of advertisers - who pays, who requires it - it is obvious. Public television revenues are more or less 70% in proportion commercial funds up to twenty-several percent of public funds. This phenomenon on a European scale, in other countries these proportions are basically the opposite. This results in the likeness of public media strategy to advertising tactics consisting in maintaining as many viewers as possible at all times. In this way, programmes recorded only for certain groups of society are excluded, and such programmes are to be guaranteed by public media. This is due, among other things, to the fact that in Poland already only - as has already been said - 8% all households pay a subscription fee on time. Almost half of households have not registered their receivers and remain outside the records and the possibility of effective debt recovery. In its judgment of 9 September 2004, the Constitutional Tribunal held that the licence fee as a public tribute must comply with the principles of universality, justice and equality resulting from the Constitution. So how far away we are from these constitutional values and somehow the concern for the implementation of the judgments of the Tribunal and for the observance of constitutional principles in this case has not been revealed. The second point is the form of organizational law, which is the form of commercial law company, which makes public media on the basis of financial results and not the programme. There is no effective way to enforce programme tasks and, consequently, the rules setting out the public mission are to a large extent legal fiction. This also causes unnecessary complexity of the management system. At the same time, the form of a public limited liability company allows to hide behind the screen the autonomy of the company or corporate secrecy particular games of interests of the persons forming part of the company's bodies and supporting their interests. As a consequence, public broadcasters with internally contradictory purposes of those declared and those actual ones lose public acceptance, lose their justification for their existence, whose purpose is a kind of service, i.e. Public mission. And what did we have instead of serving Poland and Poles? A propaganda tool. The public mission replaced serving the interests of power. The scope of personal and programme changes in public media by the PO-PSL coalition has deepened the imbalance in access to public media of various social groups. Almost everything that had right or conservative connotations disappeared from public television. As a result, there has been an unprecedented restriction on freedom of speech and freedom of public debate. A multimillion-dollar auditorium with a conservative view was deprived of its programme offer and the public had access to a full spectrum of views. This was not in line with the constitutional principles of equality vis-à-vis the law and free access to information. However, today's constitutional defenders have not been noticed here either. A few more examples: turning from Poles after 10 April 2010 and participating in building the Smoleńsk lie, promoting the historical policy of Germany by TVP. Our mothers, our fathers, the slandering soldiers of the Home Army and the lack of space on the air for creating Polish historical policy, co-financing by TVP of anti-Polish films, such as Can Poles be required to pay for defaming themselves? It is also worth recalling a very characteristic event when the Wrocław court sentenced the arrests and fines of persons blamed for disturbing the lecture of the Major. Zygmunt Bauman at the University of Wrocław. TVP's information programs manipulated the judgment that will be written in the history of the disgrace of the Polish justice system. In a supposed-free country, he punishes himself for shouting: Once with a sickle, once with a hammer a red rabble and inscriptions on his shirts: Away with the commune. And characteristic - TVP did not inform what was going on in the protest, because then we would have to tell the truth about Bauman. Although Bauman was an officer of the CWB, the criminal organization of the criminal system, the murderer of our heroes was presented only as a known professor in the world. We have also had many other examples of public media attacking the vital values of society. The peak of the misappropriation of his mission was that TVP favored one candidate during the presidential election and turned into the electoral staff of one party in the parliamentary elections. In this way the principle of worldview pluralism and impartiality was broken, which demonstrated the total submission of TVP to the then power camp, the defenders of democracy at that time was not visible either. Is it surprising, then, that public awareness has decreased on the specific role of public media in the functioning of the state? The media must be independent to be credible. This independence is significantly influenced by the way in which it is managed and financed. We therefore submit to the High House a draft law on national media, which aims to establish a system of national media. In order for these media to create a non-commercial space for culture and public conversation, they must be built on different principles, so they will be freed from the ballast form of a public limited liability company and converted into state legal entities managed on a one-man basis by directors and acting under the supervision of the National Media Council. The proposed law emphasizes the public mission of national radio and television institutions more strongly than the existing legislation so far, e.g. activities for the national community and the common good, historical awareness and knowledge of Poland abroad or the comprehensive development of the young generation and the common good and human good. Among the legal instruments aimed at guaranteeing the independence of public media, particular attention is paid to the method of financing. Without sufficient, secure and transparent funding, there is no independent public media. The financing reform is introduced by the draft Act on audiovisual contribution. There is a close correlation between the National Media Act and the Audiovisual Contribution Act. We will base the process of public media reform on their implementation, which will include the Polish Press Agency in addition to radio and television. The introduction of these two laws requires the amendment of a number of provisions in force, sometimes amendments to one law involve both the National Media Act and the Audio-Audio-Share Act. Also some transitional and adaptation provisions, e.g. concerning the first funding period of the National Media Fund, they are placed at the interface between the matter of both laws. This justifies the inclusion of provisions introducing both laws in a single separate legal act. The legislative objective of these proposals is, as I have already said, to rebuild the importance of public media, to improve organisational structures and functioning, and to strongly strengthen finance. The most important thing for the national media itself will be to regain the public's approval. Of course, the fundamental issue is the independence and professionalism of journalists and their understanding of the service. There are different levels of protection of the independence of the media, but as always many depend on people. Radiophony and television will be independent in Poland if they wish and independent people will create them if they are determined to serve the public interest. The essence of these changes is expressed in the name: national media, which is to remind that they are to be media, which are not for corporations, for one or another, but for the whole nation. That is the purpose of our project - to give public media to Poles. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The contribution will be a universal fee and the execution of this contribution will only be possible in an administrative manner. It is the electricity distributors who will be obliged to collect contributions from the final customer and to pay them to the tax authority in due time. A special account will be created at Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego, which will be powered by these funds. It will be the Public Media Fund. All the funds received by way of contributions, apart from, of course, the remuneration of the bills, will be charged to that same account. ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u356	Mrs. Marshal! Mr Fellowships and Mr Fellow Members, This is an important moment for the national council, but before I begin my speech, I would like to go ahead with two remarks. First of all, this moment. We have waited a very long time on the national council for this moment, at the moment of the emergence of new regulations on radio and television. [[Eight years.]] I do not hide that there is such a personal element here, because Minister Czabański is the main author of these laws. We've known each other a long time. This personal element may help, but it may also interfere with the assessment. Secondly, I'd like to... [[Guys.]] I wouldn't put it that way. I would like to say that the national council - and take this into account during this speech - ends its term of office. In three months, we'll have a six-year period of work. [[Voice from the room: Very good.]] So please consider the arguments, and I will defend the national council, not as a defence of our stools, our positions, but as a defence of a system, a certain system of public media established some time ago and functioning until now. In response to the request of the Marshal of the Sejm, the National Council will shortly present a detailed position on the three draft laws contained in prints 442, 443 and 444. In my speech before the High House, I will focus on issues which are essential for the democratic rule of law and fundamental rights and freedoms, in particular freedom of expression, the right to information and protection of the public interest in the media. Media law in Poland developed in conjunction with democratic changes in the political and state system, and the important stages of its creation were linked to the process of harmonisation with EU law. The national council was formed by the regulator as a body of state control and protection of the law. These regulations contain Chapter IX of the Constitution. According to Art. 213 of the Constitution, the National Radio and Television Council upholds freedom of expression, the right to information and the public interest in radio and television. The Constitutional Tribunal has repeatedly stressed in its judgments the constitutional position of the national council and the resulting legal effects. In the 2004 judgment, the Court stressed, and I quote: In accordance with the article. 213 of the Constitution and of the Act on Radio and Television, the National Council is, on the one hand, defending freedom of expression and, on the other hand, responsible for the implementation of the public mission, which is in the public interest of the radio and television service referred to in that Article. The Court added that the choice of organisational structures by which the public service mission of radio and television will be carried out belongs to the legislator, provided, however, that the body of the State, the National Council of Radiophony and Television, which is responsible for the implementation of the mission and the way in which it is carried out, is constitutionally established for this purpose. A similar view was expressed by the Court in its 2009 judgment, stating that the position of the National Council allows the latter to assume responsibility for freedom of expression, the right to information and the public interest in radio and television referred to in the provision of the Constitution cited above. It should be pointed out - the Constitutional Court stated - that, in particular, the values set out therein, such as freedom of expression or the right to information, are important for the implementation of the public service mission. In our opinion, the draft laws discussed today conflict with the above mentioned systemic tasks arising directly from the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. This is clearly demonstrated by the newly proposed regulation on fundamental issues and the consequences of their introduction, in particular the establishment of a competitive National Media Council. The solution proposed in the bill violates the constitution as it creates the so-called. competition with a constitutional body, which is the National Radio and Television Council. The appointment of the National Media Council, as provided for in Chapter 5 of the Act, is an attempt to circumvent the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. The National Media Council will consist of six members appointed by the Sejm, the Senate and the President. This structure refers directly to how the composition of the national council is to be drawn up, and the novelty is the definition of a detailed procedure for the appointment of only one of the representatives of the Sejm, to be chosen from among the three candidates designated by the largest parliamentary opposition club. This solution could and would serve democratic supervision of national media, if not for the fact that, in the six-member board, we know, resolutions are taken by an absolute majority in the presence of at least three members of the board. I would like to remind you that, with regard to the amendment of the Act on Radiophony and Television 11 years ago, the Constitutional Tribunal considered the solution adopted at that time to the effect that the President of the Republic of Poland is pointing out that the President of the Republic is in breach of the basic law. After this judgment and after the amendment of the law, the election of the President was re-elected in a collegial manner, which is also in line with the recommendations contained in the European documents. According to the current solution, the President of the National Media Council elects a Marshal of the Sejm. A member of the National Media Council may not be a representative of the executive and self-government authority, but may be a Member, a Senator or a Party Officer. That's what we've been talking about in this room. This solution becomes another denial of the independence of the body overseeing national media, and if we refer again comparatively to the constitutional principle of not joining offices and functions expressed towards the members of the National Radio and Television Council in Article. 214, that is also contrary to the ideas of the legislator in Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council concerning the European Parliament and of the Council concerning the implementation of this Regulation. 2 The basic law has made clear and unambiguous to the members of the National Council the prohibition of membership of a political party or trade union. We find regulation in a similar spirit in the European acquis. An example is the following quote from the recommendation of the Council of Europe Committee of Ministers on the independence and functions of regulatory authorities for the broadcasting media sector: "Rules governing the activities of regulatory authorities in the broadcasting sector, in particular the issue of membership, are a key element of the independence of these authorities. Therefore, they should be defined in such a way as to protect against all kinds of interference, particularly by political forces and economic interests. I continue to quote: For the above purposes specific rules on non-compliance should be laid down in order to avoid regulatory authorities being influenced by political force. The draft provides for the appointment of CEOs by the Chairman of the National Media Council on the basis of competitions. There is no information in the proposed rules on the criteria to be met by candidates for these functions and what the Council will guide in the selection of the competition. There are no instruments guaranteeing transparent and public performance of competitions, making public the assumptions and information about the conduct of the competition and the public relations involved. The appointment for the next term of office of directors of public media may already take place without a competition, and the number of term of office held is in principle unlimited. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Article 41 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the National Media Council project is equipped with the competence to deal with complaints about the activities of national media institutions and the right to evaluate draft legal acts relevant to the functioning of public media in Poland. What were the reasons for the designers, excluding from the jurisdiction of the National Council for dealing with complaints about the activities of national media institutions? The National Radio and Television Council acts in the interest of all audiences and upholds important democratic values. [[Voice from the hall: Ha, ha, ha!]] It is therefore not possible, in accordance with the applicable law, to exclude a specific group of recipients and their complaints, opinions and applications from the competence of the constitutional body. At the same time, it should be stressed that Chapter 4 of the Act on Radiophony and Television is fully repealed in the draft law, and therefore the authorisation to issue implementing acts by the National Council of Radiophony and Television, including regulations concerning the organisation of public benefit and political parties, is repealed. This decision of the projector is all the more unclear because, as I have already mentioned, in this respect the draft law on national media states that the national council receives reports from national radio and television institutions, but the proposal does not specify for what purpose, for which act and by whom specific rules will be issued for the access of eligible entities to programmes disseminated by national institutions. A very large number of eligible entities, for example, need to be reminded of this. about public benefit organisations. These regulations hang in a vacuum. We have collaborated, and effectively, for the benefit of the public with at least a few ministries. The State's audiovisual policy concerns all media service operators and recipients. It should therefore be recognised that the action taken by the national audiovisual policy-making board is based on the provision of the Article. Article 6 (6) (a) Point 1 of point 2 follows directly from the constitutional standard set out in Article. 213. In this way, not only will there be no burden on the state budget, but there will be significant savings, as the national council would be financed with 1.5% a write-off from the revenue from the contribution, not from the state budget, as is currently the case. ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u560	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a draft law on posting of workers in the framework of the provision of services contained in print no. 408. First of all, I would like to explain how the question of posting workers in the framework of the provision of services is regulated in the current legislation. Such provisions are included, in particular, in the Labour Code and the Law on State Labour Inspection and are the implementation of the provisions of Directive 96/71/EC concerning the posting of workers in the framework of the provision of services in our legal order. I will further refer to this Directive as being fundamental in contrast to Directive 2014/67/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council, the purpose of which is to enforce the Basic Directive and which is referred to as the Implementation Directive. The proposed law is precisely intended to implement the Implementation Directive, which should take place by 18 June 2016. The provisions of the basic Directive shall apply in three cases. First, when an undertaking delegates its employees to the territory of another Member State for the purpose of temporarily carrying out work related to the performance of the contract that that undertaking has concluded with the recipient of services operating in a delegated State. Secondly, when an undertaking delegates its employees to an establishment or an undertaking belonging to a group of undertakings in the territory of another Member State. Third, where temporary staff are both posted workers in the territory of another Member State and provide work to an undertaking established or operating in the territory of another Member State. In all cases, there must be an employment relationship between the posting undertaking or agency and posted workers in another Member State during the period of posting. The basic Directive lays down employment standards for posted workers who provide work in another Member State. Such regulations are currently included in the provisions of the Labour Code. The basic Directive also requires Member States to designate one or more liaison offices or other competent national authorities for administrative cooperation with their counterparts in other Member States. This is currently the role of the State Labour Inspection. The basic Directive also provides that a posted worker in the territory of another Member State should have the right to exercise his rights in the courts of that Member State relating to the conditions of work and employment guaranteed by that Directive. Such standards are currently contained in the provisions of the Civil Procedure Code. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The drafting and submission of a proposal for a law on the posting of workers in the framework of the provision of services to the High House has become necessary because of the implementation directive I have already mentioned. Let me remind you that its provisions should be implemented in the national legal order by 18 June 2016. The purpose of the Implementation Directive is to ensure that the appropriate level of protection of posted workers laid down in the Basic Directive is respected. The proposed law therefore contains solutions for both the basic directive and the implementation directive, thus creating a comprehensive regulation relating to the posting of workers in the framework of the provision of services. At the same time, they should facilitate the exercise of the freedom to provide services by the service provider and promote fair competition and thus support the functioning of the internal market. I will now come to the discussion of the scope of the proposed law, as well as the presentation of the basic solutions contained in it. First of all, I would like to explain that the proposed bill contains definitions of the terms used in it. This should ensure greater transparency and readability of the proposed regulation. I would like to pay particular attention to the definition of an employer posting an employee from the territory of the Republic of Poland and the definition of a posted worker from the territory of our country. In both cases, a worker is referred to within the meaning of the law of the country to which the posting takes place. A person who is not an employee within the meaning of the Labour Code may be an employee within the meaning of the law of the State to which he will be sent. Thus, such a person will be covered by the provisions of the Basic and Implementation Directive, which will be in practice, for example. means the obligation to provide such a person with certain employment conditions in force in the country to which he will be posted, even though in the light of Polish legislation he will not be an employee, but for example. A contractor. As regards the subjective scope of the proposed law, it is assumed that the law will not apply to merchant navy companies in respect of crews on merchant ships, which is in accordance with Article. 2 (2) 1 the basic Directive and international transport because of the specific nature of the operations carried out in such transport. On the other hand, cabotage will be an exception to which the proposed law will apply, but to a limited extent due to the specificities of such services. The information relates to this scope of the Act I will start by presenting the obligations of employers posting workers on the territory of the Republic of Poland. There are three kinds of responsibilities. Firstly, these are information and administrative obligations related to the intention of posting workers in our country and the temporary performance of such workers in that territory. In this respect, the proposed law obliges the employer, firstly, to designate a person to mediate with the State Labour Inspection and to send and receive documents or notices. Such person will have to reside in the territory of the Republic of Poland during the period of posting. Secondly, the transmission of the State Labour Inspection at its request to the person authorised to represent the employer during the inspection carried out by the State Labour Inspection. Thirdly, notification of the State Labour Inspection of the intention of posting workers on the territory of the Republic of Poland. The relevant statement in this case will contain a number of information detailed in the proposed law. The employer will also inform the State Labour Inspection of any change in the identity of the employer, the contact person with the State Labour Inspection and the place where the employee records are kept, as well as the basic data identifying the user's employer if posted workers do their job for him. Fourthly, the storage during the period of posting of basic documents relating to posted workers, including copies of contracts of employment or other equivalent documents certifying the conditions of employment of posted workers, the working time documentation on the start and end of work and the number of hours worked, as well as documents relating to the amount of remuneration, deductions from that remuneration and evidence of the payment of remuneration of employees. Fifthly, the provision of the State Labour Inspection at its request of the above documents during the period of posting and their translation into Polish. Delivery of the State Labour Inspection at its request of the above documents or translation into Polish after the end of the posting, but for a period of not more than 2 years. As regards other obligations relating to the provision of certain conditions of employment for posted workers in Poland, the law contains the following solutions. It contains the regulations currently contained in the Labour Code, making the necessary corrections and clarifications in order to achieve full consistency with the provisions of the Basic Directive. It introduces the principle of joint and several liability for certain outstanding debts owed to a posted worker on the territory of the Republic of Poland who provides work related to construction works or maintenance of a building. It will be jointly and severally responsible for the outstanding minimum remuneration, as well as the outstanding remuneration and overtime allowance, the employer posting the worker to our country and the entity entrusting such an employer with the execution of works related to the construction work or maintenance of the building. However, the entrusted entity will be able, under certain conditions, to be free of liability. As regards further control obligations which the State Labour Inspection will be required to carry out at the request of the competent authority and other Member States, in this respect, at the request of the State Labour Inspection, the employer or the person acting on his behalf will be required to provide information, documents or declarations on matters relating to the posting of workers on the territory of the Republic of Poland. Turning to the provisions of the proposed law on the rights and obligations of the State Labour Inspection, which will perform the duties of the competent authority within the meaning of the Implementation Directive, I would like to thank all the representatives of the inspection, who have been very involved in the drafting of the proposed act. I particularly thank the Chief Labour Inspector for his favour and understanding of the role of the State Labour Inspection in the proposed law. The tasks assigned to the State Labour Inspection will serve to achieve the overarching objective of implementing the Implementation Directive into the Polish legal order. Therefore, their implementation should contribute to promoting fair competition and promoting the functioning of the internal market. The National Labour Inspection will carry out five types of tasks. Firstly, it will be information tasks carried out, so far, by providing, at the request of the interested party, information on the conditions of employment of posted workers in the territory of the Republic of Poland, as well as in the new formula, i.e. by maintaining and updating a dedicated website. This website will contain all information useful for the practical application of the provisions relating to the posting of workers in the framework of the provision of services. In addition to information on legal regulations in this area, it also contains contact details of the person responsible for providing information on employment conditions guaranteed to posted workers in the territory of the Republic of Poland, as well as on the entities responsible for providing information on national law and practice on the rights and obligations of employers posting workers to the territory of the Republic of Poland and on the rights and obligations of such workers. In addition, an auxiliary model of the statement to be made by an employer wishing to delegate workers to the territory of the Republic of Poland will be provided on the website. Secondly, the State Labour Inspection in the framework of information cooperation with the competent authorities of other Member States will inform those authorities of: (a) the conditions of employment of posted workers in the territory of the Republic of Poland, (b) irregularities detected relating to the posting of workers in or from the territory of the Republic of Poland, as well as offences committed by the employer of the posting of a worker in our territory or by the employer of the posting of a worker from the territory of our country, (c) the posting of workers within or from the territory of the Republic of Poland in response to the reasoned requests of those bodies. The preparation of responses to reasoned requests from the competent authorities of other Member States will require the State Labour Inspection to cooperate, inter alia. with employers posting an employee from the territory of the Republic of Poland, the Social Insurance Institution, the heads of tax offices and other public administrations. In view of the need to respond to a reasoned request within 25 working days of receipt, the draft law provides that the cooperating entities will be required to provide information on their activities within a time limit allowing inspection to prepare a summary response. At the same time, in urgent cases involving the provision of information contained in national registers, the National Labour Inspection will provide replies within 2 working days. Thirdly, the State Labour Inspection will have the right to carry out checks on the posting of workers. In this respect, the proposed law entitles inspection to check the regularity of posting of workers on the territory of the Republic of Poland, when there are doubts as to whether the worker concerned can be regarded as a posted worker in the territory of our country. Such control may concern two aspects: firstly, whether an employer posting an employee on the territory of the Republic of Poland actually carries out significant activities in the territory of another Member State, other than management or administrative activities of a purely internal nature; secondly, whether a worker seconded to the territory of the Republic of Poland carries out his work in that territory It's only temporary. At the same time, the proposed law gives an example of what issues should be taken into account when assessing the regularity of posting. The State Labour Inspection will also be able, using all the legal resources it has, to control the conditions of employment of posted workers in our country. Fourthly, the proposed law provides for significant powers for the State Labour Inspection in the field of labour court proceedings and in the enforcement of criminal liability from an employer posting an employee to our country or from such territory in the event of a breach of certain provisions of the proposed law. Thus, labour inspectors will have the right to bring an action before the labour court to determine the law applicable to the employment relationship between the worker and his employer if it is established that the worker concerned cannot be regarded as a posted worker in the territory of the Republic of Poland. The State Labour Inspection will be able to make such findings, for example. in the course of checking the regularity of posting of workers on the territory of the Republic of Poland, when he finds that such a worker is working continuously in Poland and not only temporarily. The proposed law also grants the State Labour Inspection powers of a public prosecutor in cases of misconduct set out in the Act. Penalizations will be subject to violation of certain obligations by the employer of the posting of a worker on the territory of the Republic of Poland related to notification of the intention of posting workers to Poland, as well as the course of work carried out by the employee on our territory. Fifthly, the State Labour Inspection in the framework of cooperation on cross-border enforcement of administrative fines or administrative fines will undertake, at the request of the applicant authority, activities related to: 1. notify the employer of the posting of a worker from the territory of the Republic of Poland of the decision to impose an administrative penalty or administrative fine on him in respect of a breach of the provisions on posting of workers, 2. with notification to such an employer of the application for the execution of such a penalty or fine. I would like to pay a little more attention to these tasks. Regulation on cross-border enforcement of administrative fines or administrative fines is intended to effectively and rapidly implement decisions or decisions imposing such penalties or fines on employers posting workers to another Member State where they breach the obligations relating to posting. As I have already mentioned, in this respect, the State Labour Inspection will carry out two kinds of tasks. First, it will be the activities related to notifying the employer of the posting of a worker from the territory of the Republic of Poland of a decision on the imposition of an administrative penalty or administrative fine on him. We propose that, in the event of such a request, the State Labour Inspection take all necessary steps to notify the employer without undue delay of the decision to impose an administrative penalty or administrative fine on him. Such notification should take place no later than one month after receipt of the request from the applicant authority. The State Labour Inspection, after checking whether the application for notification complies with the formal requirements, whether it contains information corresponding to the content of the decision imposing the fine, and whether the documents indicated in the application have been attached thereto, shall forward to the employer a decision imposing a fine or fine on it and the documents accompanying it. On the other hand, if the application does not comply with the formal requirements, the inspection shall return the application to the applicant authority. Secondly, it will be the enforcement of an administrative penalty or administrative fine imposed on the employer of the posting of a worker from the territory of the Republic of Poland. In this respect, the proposed law divides the tasks between the State Labour Inspection and the heads of tax offices. The labour inspection will carry out all activities prior to the transfer of the application for enforcement to the head of the tax office, while the enforcement of the amounts due will be carried out by the competent enforcement authorities, i.e. by the chiefs of the IRS. It will therefore be the task of the State Labour Inspection to check the application formally and to determine whether it contains any information relating to the imposition of a penalty or fine, in particular whether a decision or decision has been served on the party in this case and that such a decision or decision is no longer subject to further appeal. Cross-border enforcement of administrative fines or administrative fines may only concern cases of final decisions or decisions. Therefore, if the application is complete, the State Labour Inspection shall notify the employer no later than one month after receipt of the application. A positively verified application, together with the implementing title attached thereto, will also be handed over to the head of the tax office, unless the debtor has previously made a voluntary payment. On the other hand, if the proposal does not meet the formal requirements or does not contain the necessary information, the labour inspection will return the application to the applicant authority. The State Labour Inspection will be able to return the application if the overall amount of the penalty or fine is less than EUR 350 and if the decision to impose a penalty or fine is contrary to the fundamental principles of the legal order of the Republic of Poland. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! We also provide for regulations to suspend or suspend cross-border enforcement of fines or administrative fines. This will apply to cases where an interested party makes use of exceptional measures to amend final decisions or decisions on the imposition of such a penalty or fine before the authority of the State in which such a penalty or fine is imposed. The State Labour Inspection will be the creditor of the charges in question. That is why we are proposing provisions that will make it easier to fulfil this role. These are changes in the provisions of the Enforcement Act in the administration. As a creditor, the State Labour Inspection will be able, in some situations, to waive the cash claims sought by applying the relevant provisions of the Public Finance Act. It will also be possible, at the debtor's request, to postpone the repayment of all or part of the receivable or to spread the payment of all or part of the receivable into instalments. In accordance with the Implementation Directive, the enforced debts will constitute revenue for the State budget. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u562	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice in relation to the presented bill in print no. 408. The draft law, submitted by the Minister of Family, Labour and Social Policy, on the posting of workers in the framework of the provision of services adopted by the Council of Ministers, introduces into the Polish legal order solutions resulting from the EU Directive 2014/67, as well as adopted so far in other legal acts, including. in the Labour Code, the arrangements implementing the EU Directive 96/71, creating one comprehensive legal act relating to the posting of workers in the framework of the provision of services. The EU Directive 96/71/EC, which has been in force for many years, indicates, inter alia, what working conditions of the host country must be provided for posted workers in the course of their work in that country. The new directive, the implementation of which is the draft law in question, aims in particular at strengthening the enforcement of the rules already in force. In this context, it is worth pointing out that the posting of workers takes place within the framework of the freedom to provide services. It is necessary to distinguish between posting within the framework of this freedom and the principle of free movement of workers, which gives every citizen the right to move freely to another Member State for the purpose of taking up employment and residence in that State and protects them against discrimination as regards employment, remuneration and other working and employment conditions compared to nationals of that Member State. On the other hand, the posted worker is only in another country for a limited period of time, i.e. the duration of the work, does not integrate with the labour market of that country and returns to its country after completion of the work. The purpose of the directive being implemented is to better protect the rights of posted workers in the framework of cross-border provision of services, in particular the enforcement of the employment conditions applicable in the Member State where the service is to be provided, in accordance with Article. 3 Directive 96/71/EC - while facilitating the exercise of the freedom to provide services by service providers and promoting fair competition between service providers. The Directive aims to improve the efficiency of the enforcement of minimum employment conditions in the Member State where the work is to be provided, inter alia. such as standards and working time, daily and weekly rest periods, rest periods, health and safety. In practice, it should eliminate circumvention of the existing rules on posting of workers, while respecting the principle of freedom to provide services. The new Act is to enter into force on 18 June 2016. This is because the Member States of the European Union must adopt by that date the laws, regulations and administrative provisions necessary to implement Directive 2014/67/EU. In view of the provisions of the Implementation Directive, the draft Posting of Workers Act in the framework of the provision of services has been defined inter alia. principles: the protection of posted workers from Poland and our country, the control of compliance with the rules on posting of workers, the cooperation of the State Labour Inspection with the competent authorities of other Member States concerning the posting of workers, the cross-border enforcement of administrative fines or administrative fines. The new regulation takes over from the Labour Code provisions concerning employment conditions, which must be guaranteed to posted workers during their work in Poland. An employer posting an employee to our country should provide such an employee with the conditions of employment as set out in the Act concerning, inter alia,: standards and working time, the dimension of rest leave, minimum wage for work, health and safety at work. If a posted worker from Poland or Poland exercises the right to initiate administrative or judicial proceedings against the employer, it will be protected against adverse treatment by the employer. The draft law also regulates the obligations of an employer from another Member State posting a worker to Poland. The draft law also sets out the tasks of the State Labour Inspection in relation to the posting of workers in the framework of the provision of services. State Labour Inspection, among others. It will work with its counterparts from other Member States, e.g. will inform about the conditions of employment of workers posted to Poland. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The powers of the State Labour Inspection have also been strengthened.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u566	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present an opinion on the Government's draft law on posting of workers in the framework of the services provided, contained in the Seym printing No 408. Today's project is one of the results of our country's membership of the European Union. The preparation of the directive, which should be reflected in Polish law, was attended by experts, MEPs and officials representing our country. The explanatory memorandum to the Act states that implementation of the legislation will improve, among others. access to information on the conditions of employment in a given country and, consequently, workers posted abroad will be better protected, employers will gain more transparent rules and state institutions will have a facilitated exercise of their control functions vis-à-vis the labour market as a whole. In this way, I would like to remind you that Poland is a member of the European Union, co-creating the rules in force in Poland, not a country that is only implementing the recommendations of Brussels. Undoubtedly, the opinion of the Platform, as well as the social partners assessing the solutions proposed by the government, needs this project. As I said, it regulates a number of unclear issues relating to the posting of workers to work abroad. However, some of the detailed provisions contained in the Act require a broader discussion. Such a problem is, among others. on the deadlines for employers presenting documentation at the request of the State Labour Inspection. It follows from the provisions that the inspection itself has 25 working days to provide information to the competent authorities of another country regarding the situation in the undertaking concerned, whereas the trader himself has only 7 days - previously proposed 5 days - to prepare and transmit the inspection of the relevant documentation. The Social Insurance Institution listed in the Act has 10 working days, so why does the entity that has to collect documents have much less time than the institution that only has to pass these documents on to its counterparts in another country? These issues should be clarified. Of course, most of them will be able to handle such a short period of time when they will require regulations to do so. However, as a law, we should remember that entrepreneurs create jobs and we should interfere as little as possible with the functioning of economic operators. However, it seems that short deadlines, for which failure to comply with sanctions threatens, may be an unnecessary element hindering business activity. This situation leads to another problem. Perhaps the labour inspection, which is responsible for the functioning of the law in practice, needs more time to transmit documents due to staff problems. The explanatory memorandum states that the possible increase in costs associated with changes in the State Labour Inspection will be within the allocated budget for 2016 and will not constitute a basis for applying for additional funding from the State budget to carry out the tasks in question later. We'd like to know exactly what that means. Does the inspection have additional resources to carry out the new tasks, or will it have to develop them? Will new officials be needed, and in what number? These issues should also be clarified. Finally, I will also mention the problem of the inclusion of employees of transport companies and cabotage. There are even more doubts, but we will certainly talk about everything during the procession. As I said, this proposal, which is the implementation of the European Parliament directive, is positive. However, due to some ambiguities concerning specific solutions on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform asks that the project be directed to further work in the parliamentary committees. ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u568	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Poselski Club15, I have the honour to present my position on the government proposal for the Posting of Workers Act in the framework of the provision of services. The proposed law aims to implement the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 15 May 2014 on the enforcement of Directive 96/71 concerning the posting of workers in the framework of the provision of services. The current regulations were included in Chapter IIa of Section 2 of the Labour Code.Terms of employment of workers assigned to work in the territory of the Republic of Poland from a country which is a member of the European Union, as well as in the Act on State Labour Inspection. Directive 96/71 of the European Parliament and of the Council, known as the Basic Directive, regulates situations where workers are temporarily posted by their employer to another Member State in the framework of services provided in that State. According to Art. Article 23 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Directive 2014/67 requires Member States to adopt, by 18 June 2016, the laws, regulations and administrative provisions necessary to comply with it. As we read in the explanatory memorandum to the draft law, Directive 2014/67, whose implementation is to be ensured by this draft law, aims primarily at ensuring that the appropriate level of protection of the rights of posted workers is respected for the purpose of the cross-border provision of services set out in the basic directive. This includes, in particular, the enforcement of minimum employment conditions applicable in the Member State where the service is to be provided, while facilitating the exercise of the freedom to provide services by service providers and promoting fair competition between service providers and thereby supporting the functioning of the internal market. In conclusion, since the proposed law consequently implies the inclusion in a single legal act of both the provisions implementing the Basic Directive by transferring them from the laws in force and the establishment of new regulations implementing Directive 2014/67, the Poselski Kukiz Club15 does not comment on the proposed proposal. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u570	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I am proud to present my position on the draft Posting of Workers Act in the framework of the provision of services submitted by the Minister of Family, Labour and Social Policy. In the view of the applicants, the new regulation aims to better protect the rights of posted workers in the provision of services while respecting the principle of freedom to provide these services. The members of the Modern Club support the main idea contained in the proposed bill aimed at clarifying the provisions in order to ensure better protection of workers' rights. However, the detailed analysis of the draft provisions raises numerous reservations about the coherence of the proposals, so further intensive work on the text is desirable. The comments are editorial and strictly substantive. In plenary, we would like to signal some of the substantive doubts which are of fundamental importance in the context of the posting of workers in the framework of the provision of services under Directive 2014/67/EU. Case one. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 3 refers to a temporary worker for work, but there is no clarification on how the concept of temporary work should be treated. This is so important that in accordance with Article. 14 of this law The State Labour Inspection is to have the authority to check and assess whether the posted worker is temporarily working in Poland. We believe that this requires clarification in order to limit disputes on the part of employers and the State Labour Inspection when interpreting this provision. The second problem concerns the intention of accompanying the wording of the rules on joint and several liability. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 7 this type of responsibility, i.e. both the employer and the person entrusting the work, was foreseen for the project submitted. It raises doubts as to whether the purpose of this provision is indeed to apply joint and several liability only to this narrow category of works related to construction works and maintenance of the building, which seems to be unreasonable, as there is no justification for such special protection only for workers carrying out specific work and at the same time refusing to grant such protection to other workers. In our view, the purpose of this provision should in principle be to protect all workers covered by the Act. The third problem concerns an incomprehensible distinction in Article. Article 12 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 and 5 of the draft act on time limits for the provision of information to the State Labour Inspection concerning the activities listed in the draft entities and necessary to be included in response to requests referred to in Article. Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 point (4) (a) d. The posting employer and the trader are obliged to provide information within 7 days, while the Social Insurance Institution, the heads of tax offices and other public administrations are obliged to provide information within 10 days. Although the deadline has been extended from 5 to 7 days for employers and traders, the question remains to be raised as to the grounds for distinguishing between the deadlines followed by applicants. The proposals put forward can be regarded as an unjustified advantage for public administrations vis-à-vis private operators. Another important question concerns the discrepancy in the concept of posted workers in the territory of the Republic of Poland and posted workers from the territory of the Republic of Poland. The first applies to the definition of a worker in force under Polish law, while the second applies to the definition in force in the Member State concerned. In the first case, the employee is a person employed on the basis of a contract of employment, appointment, selection, appointment or cooperative employment contract, so any other form of employment, e.g. on the basis of a contract order. In the case of a second employee under Union law, there is a person who fulfils three basic conditions: he/she provides work of specific economic value to another entity, is subject to instructions issued by another entity and, in return for the work he/she receives remuneration and under the law of another Member State, employees may be employed under civil law contracts. Further comments concern the inconsistent use of time limits. There should be no consequence in a law of the rank of a law. In general, it would be advisable to use terminology corresponding to the nomenclature K.p.c. The case concerns, in particular, the terms set out in Article. 3 (variably the territory of the Republic of Poland or the territory of the Republic of Poland) and art. 31 (the term "citizen' should be replaced by "natural persons'.) As you can see, the comments are numerous and essential. I only introduced the most important of them. [[♪ The bell ♪]] In view of the above arguments, the Modern Posel Club, supporting the main idea, the idea contained in the proposed bill, will vote in favour of directing this project to further work in committees. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

In Poland, the role of such an office is played by the State Labour Inspection. In Poland, this obligation has been fulfilled by art. 1103 § 2 of the Civil Procedure Code according to which the employee's actions concerning the provision of conditions of employment in accordance with Article. 671 and Art. 672 Labour Code belongs to national jurisdiction also when the employee is either directed to work in the territory of the Republic of Poland by an employer established in a State which is a member of the European Union. ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u592	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! This draft law replaces the Energy Efficiency Act of 15 April 2012 in view of the need to implement Directive 2012/27/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 25 October 2012 on energy efficiency, which obliges Member States to achieve the climate and energy targets for 2020. The draft law responds to the raised demands on the need to continue and strengthen the current system to support energy savings, especially in the area of energy end-use. The implementation of the proposed solutions will result in improved energy efficiency of the Polish economy and also in ensuring that the 2020 targets are met. The implementation of the legal regulations contained in the bill will contribute to improving energy efficiency and innovation of the Polish economy. The implementation of energy efficiency projects outside measurable energy savings will also stimulate the competitiveness of Polish companies on the European market, especially those which are highly energy-intensive. The scope of the Act. In particular, the Act concerns end-users, understood as natural or legal persons who purchase energy for their own use, as well as energy undertakings and providers of energy efficiency improvement measures. The draft law aims to introduce legal provisions setting out: firstly, objectives in the field of energy efficiency and energy savings for 2020 for the public sector and for the energy efficiency certificates system, secondly, the rules for drawing up national action plans, the tasks of public sector entities and the rules for implementing the obligations under the energy efficiency certificates system and carrying out the energy audit of the entrepreneur. The draft law on matter regulated by the Act requires entities to carry out an undertaking to improve energy efficiency at the final consumer, confirmed by an energy efficiency audit or to obtain and present to the President of the Energy Regulatory Office a certificate of energy efficiency, and introduces an obligation for energy audit for large enterprises, and lays down the rules for carrying out this audit. In addition, the proposed law also abolishes the obligation to tender, whereby the President of the URE chooses the energy efficiency improvement projects for which energy efficiency certificates can be obtained. This will constitute a significant simplification of the procedure for issuing energy efficiency certificates. The withdrawal of tenders is the result of negative experience in 2013 and 2014 and 2015. The draft law provides for the President of the URE to issue energy efficiency certificates at the request of the entity with which the energy efficiency improvement project will be carried out or the entity authorised by it. Such simplification should significantly speed up the issue of energy efficiency certificates. According to the draft law, energy efficiency certificates will not be issued for projects that have already been carried out. This is due to the need to ensure the compatibility of the proposed energy efficiency certificates system with the new Commission guidelines on State aid for environmental protection and energy-related objectives in 2014 and 2020 - i.e. 2012/C 200/01 - and in particular with the need to ensure the so-called. the incentive effect of recognising the aid for energy purposes as compatible with the internal market. Expected effects of the Energy Efficiency Act. Financial. The implementation of the tasks set out in the proposed law entrusted to the President of the URE should be carried out in a manner that is as non-financially burdensome as possible to the State budget, which means that it is possible to finance within the expenditure limit set annually in the Budget Act in the appropriate part of the budget, without having to allocate funds above that limit. Other effects. The government energy efficiency project should contribute to the effectiveness of the current energy efficiency certification system, so-called. white certificates, thanks to the simplification of existing procedures and the limitation of the possibility for obliged entities to fulfil their obligations by paying a substitute fee. Reinforcing energy efficiency mechanisms will ensure rational use of natural resources in order to reduce negative impacts on the environment. The introduction of the draft Law on Standards should serve the full and correct implementation of Directive 2012/27/EU and ensure the required improvement of energy efficiency in Poland. In view of the fact that the European Commission opened formal proceedings against Poland on 22 October 2015, i.e. has been forwarded pursuant to Article. 258 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union, a reasoned opinion on the absence of a notification of measures for transposition into national law of the Directive, which I mentioned here, is appropriate to adopt this proposal as soon as possible. Otherwise, there is a real risk that the European Commission will bring an action before the Court of Justice of the European Union for punishment. Thank you very much. [[Thank you.]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u598	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Kukiz Club15 on the draft Energy Efficiency Act submitted to the High House. It should be made clear at the outset that the law in this form is the consequence of the introduction of European Union legislation into Polish law, the successive stages of the climate and energy package. We are facing a decline in the competitiveness of the European economy vis-à-vis other countries. We are dealing with a high level of energy intensity in Poland. The level of energy intensity must be reduced, that is a fact, but will it affect the lower cost of manufacturing? I'm having second thoughts here. Due to the entry into force of the above-mentioned energy price package for final customers in Poland will increase in the opinion of experts. Billions will have to be paid for CO2 allowances. For the record, the package sets out three main objectives. The first objective is to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 20%, the second - 20% share of renewables in total energy consumption in EU countries, and the third - to increase energy efficiency by 20%. We should also refer to the climate agreement signed in New York on Saturday, 22 April. The signed Paris Agreement of 2015 must ratify all countries. I would remind you that the agreement is expected to enter into force by the end of 2020 at the latest, when the emission reduction targets start to apply. Unfortunately, the agreement provides for systematic elimination of fossil fuels, including coal, from an energy mix. The Government of the Republic of Poland explicitly focuses on new low-carbon fossil fuel combustion technologies. And in general this is a very difficult situation for the Polish economy. The level of legal volatility for the energy-related economic sector is also unpredictable from a business point of view, and this affects energy. Construction of modern manufacturing units as well as comprehensive modernization of existing blocks significantly improve the energy efficiency of Polish power plants, e.g. investment in the modernisation of boilers in the blocks in the Turów power plant, construction of the flue gas denitrification plant in the Lower Odra Power Plant or the flue gas desulfurization system in the Bydgoszcz power plant. Energy efficiency is the ratio of energy savings compared to energy consumed or forecasted. At this point, it is about reducing energy consumption during production, during operation or in the provision of services. For example, in the building this applies to the energy needed for heating, cooling, ventilation, air conditioning or electricity used by any device. The Act as proposed by the Government of the Republic of Poland introduces the provisions of the Directive of 25 October 2012 and sets out: principles for the development of a national energy efficiency action plan, tasks of public sector entities in the field of energy efficiency, the principles of implementation of the obligation to obtain energy savings, the principles of carrying out the energy audit of the undertaking. This directive also defines energy efficiency issues in line with the policy of the European Union and Poland is committed to establishing indicative national targets for energy efficiency. energy efficiency for 2020, based on primary energy consumption or final consumption. You bet on, among other things.: renovating at least 3% of the total area of central government buildings per year; establishing national and long-term strategies to promote investment in renovation of residential, commercial buildings, and developing national energy efficiency or equivalent systems to ensure energy savings for final customers of at least 1,5% annual; in addition, the use of high-efficiency cogeneration and efficient central heating and cooling installations; mandatory and regular energy audits carried out at least every 4 years in large enterprises, except those where certified systems are implemented; encouraging consumers to use energy more cost-effectively through the dissemination of smart grids and meters and providing accurate information on energy bills. I'm moving on to the definition of the most important. An energy efficiency audit shall mean a study containing an analysis of energy consumption and specifying the technical state of the facility, technical equipment or installation, including a list of projects to improve the energy efficiency of the facility, technical equipment or installation, and an assessment of their economic viability and energy savings. Energy efficiency is the ratio of the output of the facility, technical equipment or installation, under typical conditions of use or operation, to the amount of energy consumed on the facility. The requirements for energy efficiency audit under the white certificate system are laid down in the Law Regulation. The emphasis here is on the assessment of the technical condition and the analysis of the energy consumption of the facility. The so-called system. White certificates have been in force in Poland since 2013. Energy companies selling electricity, heat or natural gas to final customers are required to obtain and present for redemption to the President of the Energy Regulatory Office, URE, energy efficiency certificate or payment of a substitute fee. White certificates are generally a confirmation of saving a certain amount of energy as a result of investments to improve energy efficiency. White certificates have property rights and are traded on a commodity energy exchange. The savings to be certified in Poland are primary energy for 1 year, and the entities required for certification are energy companies selling electricity, natural gas, network heat to end-users, end-users, commodity brokerage houses and brokerage houses purchasing electricity on the stock exchange on behalf of customers, without installations covered by the EU-ETS system. For the record, ww. responsibilities may be carried out by projects at the end-user to improve energy efficiency, to purchase energy efficiency certificates or to pay a substitute fee. In the URE project, the regulator issues certificates on request to companies that have implemented energy saving projects, and they are only for new projects. To date, there is a complex, difficult, costly tender procedure. The Kukiz Club15 supports the project, but provided that it should be referred to the relevant committees for consideration and further work. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u640	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! A few days ago, on 22 April 2016 in Ostrowiec Świętokrzyski the celebrations of the 60th anniversary of the oldest municipal company, the Municipal Communication Company took place. During the 60 years of its existence, the Ostrowiec MPK functioned in various organizational forms and passed the changing turns of fate. Its origins were extremely modest. In April 1956, public public transport was carried out by six buses operating two lines, the length of which was only 13 km. The fact that in the first year of operation, red buses carried around 1.4 million passengers shows how much public bus traffic was needed at the time. The increase in the number of inhabitants of Ostrowiec Świętokrzyski, the construction of new housing estates and the creation of new workplaces, including the new steelworks of the Ostrowiec steelworks, made the Municipal Communication Company strong, employing more than 500 employees, with nearly 130 buses. On 58 whole-day lines and 43 peak lines were transported on average 147 thousand at the time. passengers a day. The length of the routes was 300 km. It was the time of the company's greatest development. The last few years have been a difficult time for the company. The lack of funding caused the ageing of the bus rolling stock. The decrease in passenger numbers and competition from private carriers resulted in a decrease in the number of transport lines which were only limited at some point to the area of the city. The fact that the company survived is mainly due to its employees, which are currently around 100. Today, the Olympic MPK has 35 buses serving 10 all-day lines and two peak lines. The fact that the last few months have brought stability to the situation in the company is an optimism. Its manifestation is the progressive renewal of the rolling stock for a slightly newer one, produced after 2000 buses. This was made possible by means of funds transferred to the company by the owner, which is the municipality of Ostrowiec Świętokrzyski. It is important for the inhabitants that some of the suburban communication lines have been restored. This resulted in an increase in the number of passengers carried. The Management Board of the Municipal Communication Company and the authorities of Ostrowiec Świętokrzyski set themselves very ambitious goals for the coming years. The main objective is to provide residents of Ostrowiec Świętokrzyski and neighbouring municipalities with modern and secure public transport, which is to guarantee the exchange of buses for completely new, low-floor buses, monitored and equipped with a passenger information system. The funds for their purchase are from the regional operational programme. All this thanks to the accession of Ostrowiec Świętokrzyskie to the agreement on the Tricity over the Stone, including Skarżysko and Starachowice. Let us hope that the purchase of new 15 and 20 buses will become a fact. High Izbo! At this point, I would like to express my appreciation for the subsequent boards and all employees of the Ostrowice Municipal Communication Company. Over the 60 years of its existence, it has played and continues to play a great role in the lives of its inhabitants. It allows them to reach schools and businesses safely. It is also a significant work facility that provides a source of livelihood for many families. Therefore, it is important that the plans not only for maintenance but also for further development of the company can be realized. This is what I wish for the crew, management and local authorities of Ostrowiec Świętokrzyski. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

Wojciech Korfanty was born on 20 April 1873 in Sadzawka, a labor settlement, today the Siemianowice Silesian district, in the family of Józef Korfantego, a miner, and Karolina from Klechów, as the oldest of five children. He grew up in an ethnic Polish environment. He started school at the folk school. Further education continued in the middle school in Katowice, from which for his anti-German activities and the pro-Polish posture just before graduation he was relegated and burdened with the so-called. A wolf passport. This deepened young Wojciech's sense of patriotism. As he himself said: The merit of my national awareness I must assign to hakatistic professors in middle school, who disgusted everything Polish and Catholic, aroused curiosity in me in the Polish book, from which I wanted to learn what this debased and humiliated nation, whose language in my family I spoke. And I loved my people, my past, and his sum, and felt his son. On the school bench I tried to instill in my colleagues, as I did at home in Polish speaking, love for Poland and pride in her past. Without contact with the youth in other districts, I started secret circles in middle school where we read in the history of the nation, in the masterpieces of our poets, we learned to speak and write correctly in Polish. He passed his math extremely well when he was 22. He then started his studies at the University of Technology, and in 1898 he moved to Wrocław, where he studied law and economics. Korfanty was a brilliant political career. He began to publish the Polish magazine "Górnoślązak." In 1903, he won the Reichstag elections and entered the Polish circle bravely as the first Member from Upper Silesia. It was a turning point in Silesian history. Wojciech Korfanty experienced many injustices in his life on the part of those who ruled the Second Common Era, including imprisonment and emigration, but he always believed in Poland for which he devoted his entire life. The message of Wojciech Korfanty to the Silesian people, which was placed on the every corner of his monument on pl. Sejm Śląskiego in Katowice: One thing I ask of the Silesian people, namely to be faithful to his Christian principles and to his attachment to Poland, so that he does not cease in his work and dedication to make Poland worthy of our dreams, Poland great, power, Catholic Poland, the rule of law and always fair. What a reply, Wojciech Korfanty's request to the Silesian people is valid today, when various extreme groups, false prophets undermine Silesian Polishness, distort the truth about Silesian uprisings. Let us resist those who divide and antagonize the Silesian people. Let us not let the blood victims of our heroic forefathers fighting against the German aggressor for Silesia's Polishness. Wojciech Korfanty died on 17 August 1939 in Warsaw, shortly after his release from prison. His funeral, which took place in Katowice on August 20, 1939, turned into a great manifestation, thousands of Silesians saying goodbye to their beloved leader. He was buried in Katowice in a cemetery on the street. French. Wojciech Korfanty lived his entire life sacrificially in constant service to Silesia and Poland. Through his love of his homeland and great sacrifice, he made part of Upper Silesia return to Poland.
