ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u644	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! Today we celebrate the World Day of Safety and Health at Work and the International Day of Remembrance of Victims of Accidents at Work and Occupational Diseases. The celebration, celebrated annually on 28 April, was proclaimed in 2003 by the International Labour Organisation in cooperation with the World Health Organisation and the International Confederation of Trade Unions. The aim of this day is to remind you that promoting and creating a culture of safe work can reduce the number of people who have died in connection with working conditions. The celebrations are dedicated to the memory of workers who have suffered accidents at work, have suffered occupational diseases or have died in the course of their work. They are also an opportunity to promote labour safety issues, forms and ways of avoiding risks and protecting workers in the work process. On 28 April, therefore, it is both a day to mention the victims and an opportunity to disseminate knowledge of occupational safety problems. In the past, on 28 April, trade unions organised large rallies, industry strikes, educational and lobbying events and information campaigns. In many countries it is also an opportunity to organize demonstrations, protests and information campaigns. According to the International Labour Organisation and the European Agency for Safety and Health at Work, there are 270 million accidents at work every year, of which 350 000. it's fatal, it's almost $1,000. a day. Each year in Poland, about 300 people die from accidents at work, and another 600 suffer serious injury due to severe accidents at work. The National Labour Inspection reports show that among the main causes of accidents are: inappropriate organisation of work, lack of health and safety training, and poor health of psychophysical workers and workers caused by too long working time and fatigue. Ladies and gentlemen! The above data should be a starting point for creating more effective programmes for building a safety culture at work in Poland, based on the commitment and cooperation of all stakeholders: employers, health and safety services, state institutions and employees' representatives, including trade unions and social labour inspectors. A world day of health and safety at work and the International Day of Remembrance of Accident Victims at Work and Occupational Diseases can be an opportunity to make employers aware of the responsibility they bear for their employees, especially in times of globalisation. This is an opportunity for action to improve public health, increase the level of health and safety at work, the state of which is an indicator of quality of life and health of most people in every society. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The beginnings of Bydgoszcz date back to the first half of the 11th century, since this border fortress protected the Polish Kingdom from the invasions of Pomerania, Prussians and later the Teutonic Knights who took it for several years. Shortly after the recovery of the castle, on April 19, 1346, Kazimierz Wielki issued an act of location of the new city of Bydgoszcz on Magdeburg law. Bydgoszcz has a very complex history. She had blooming seasons, but she was also devastated by wars, lootings, and plagues. It has been Germanized in recent centuries, and for its patriotic attitude its inhabitants have experienced the cruelty of Nazi invaders. It is important that the history of Bydgoszcz be made more acquainted not only with its inhabitants, as it results from experience and conclusions for the whole country. From the beginning of its history Bydgoszcz has been an important port thanks to the development of beer, grain and salt trade. By the middle of the 17th century, it was a very important old-Polish trading city due to the scale of trade. The Prussian extortioner also appreciated the city's strategic economic location by building the Bydgoszcz Canal opening the trade route to Western Europe. Built between 1773 and 1774 by 10,000. The canal is the oldest artificial waterway on Polish soil. This ensured excellent economic development for Bydgoszcz. Innovative socio-economic institutions, national customs chambers, the Trade Council and the merchant company were created. It is worth recalling these facts from the history of Bydgoszcz in the context of huge havoc and negligence in the field of inland waterway transport in Poland. The renewal and expansion of river shipping infrastructure requires a great financial effort from the state. However, it is a long-term viable and necessary investment, also for economic, tourist and environmental reasons. This was done under the pressure of a few but noisy environmental organizations, which in the interest of developed countries of Western Europe would like to make a green open-air museum from Poland. The eight-year PO-PSL governing coalition has not taken effective steps to remedy the very disadvantage of water and energy management. Henryk Sienkiewicz coat of arms Oszyk was born in 1846 in Wola Okrrzejska in Podlasie. He was from a poor noble family. After many moves at the age of 15, he and his family moved to Warsaw. As a 17 - year - old, he wanted to take part in the January Uprising, but he was not allowed to fight because of his young age. In 1864 he won his first prize for the work of Żółkiewski for the army at Cecora. At school, however, he didn't get high marks, and his best course was in humanities. After obtaining the certificate of maturity, according to the wishes of the parents of doctors, Sienkiewicz passed to the Medical Department of the Warsaw School. In time, he resigned from his medical career and went to law school. In the end, however, he moved to the philological and historical department. Sienkiewicz wrote under the nickname Lithuanians to the Polish Gazeta and Niva. In February 1876 he and a group of friends went to the United States. During this journey there was created among others. a series of reports entitled Letters from traveling to America, as well as novels such as the Lantern or the Sachem. The writer returned to Europe after two years. He then lived in London, and then stayed in Paris for a year. During that time, a novel was created: Janko Muzykant. In 1881, he married Maria Szetkiewicz, with whom he had two children. Four years after Maria was married, she died of tuberculosis. Between May 1883 and March 1884 Sienkiewicz's novel with fire and sword was printed in the Warsaw diary. Both this and other parts of the Trilogy have brought him great popularity and made him the most popular Polish writer. In 1884 the first episodes of Quo vadis were published. Six years later, Henryk Sienkiewicz released the Krzyżaków. In the 25th anniversary of creative work, the writer was appreciated by the nation. At that time he received the estate in Oblęgorek, where today the Museum of Henryk Sienkiewicz is located. In 1905, the writer received the Nobel Prize for his entire work. In his speech on this occasion, Sienkiewicz said that this privilege is particularly valuable to the son of Poland: "She was voiced dead, and here was one of thousands of proofs that she was alive. That same year he became one of the founders of the Polish School Mothers Society. He was also a member of the Secret Teaching Society in Warsaw. After the outbreak of the war, Henryk Sienkiewicz went to Switzerland, where together with Ignacy Jan Paderewski he was one of the co-founders of the Swiss General Committee for Aid to War Victims in Poland. In 1916 he received the prize of Erasmus and Anna Jerzmanowski Foundation awarded by the Polish Academy of Skills. Henryk Sienkiewicz died on 15 November 1916 in Vevey, Switzerland and was buried there. Eight years later, the writer's ashes were brought into the country and solemnly buried in the undergrounds of St. Jana in Warsaw. Because it's 170 this year. The anniversary of Henryk Sienkiewicz's birth and the 100th anniversary of his death, 2016 was established the Year of Henryk Sienkiewicz. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-28-sejm-17-2.u655	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Every year, April 22nd, Earth Day aims to promote environmental attitudes in society. The celebration programme is designed to raise awareness of the dangers facing our planet to society and to encourage ecological behaviour. The history of World Earth Day dates back to 1970, when environmental activists decided to name environmental promotion activities organised on 22 April. This initiative proved to be a great success, and it became the beginning of the annual celebrations promoted by American Senator Nelson. In 2009, the Council On April 22, the United Nations announced World Mother Earth Day. The organizers of Earth Day have a specific goal - to make politicians and citizens aware of the danger of ecosystems as a result of human activity. International Earth Day is an annual effort to promote environmental attitudes among the people of our planet. Organic organisations organise various workshops, exhibitions, numerous competitions to bring society closer to the current problems of the functioning of the modern world. April 22 is certainly the most ecological date of the year. It reminds us that we should respect our place of residence, to which we owe so much, but we rarely think about it every day. I appeal to all parliamentarians with an appeal - let us take care of our planet in the care of the future of future generations.
